The sūrah is stating, in the form, that the *prophet* is *not* *defending himself against the accusation of evil*; he is *witnessing against the idols*, in the *sense of being* *the one who calls God to witness that he is free of the ascription*, and that the *witnessing against the idols* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *accusation* is *not met by a defense of the prophet*; it is *met by a disavowal of the idols*, and that the *disavowal* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *position* is *not a* *personal* *position*; it is *the position of the one who is free of the ascription*, in the way described in *verse 31's fourth denial*.

**"That I Am Quit of That Which You Ascribe as Partners" — The Disavowal That the Sūrah States**

The *substance* of the *witnessing* is the *disavowal*: *innī barīʾun mimmā tuʿariku* — "that I am quit of that which you ascribe as partners." The *barāʾa* — the *disavowal* — is the *same* *word* the *sūrah* *used* in *verse 13* for the *Prophet's* *disassociation* from the *Makkans'* *fabrication*, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *disavowal* is *not a* *prophet's* *individual* *act*; it is *the form of the prophetic disavowal itself*, in the *sense of being* *the form that every prophet takes when he is accused by the idolaters*, in the *sense of being* *the form that separates the prophet from the ascription*. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *ʿĀd* are *hearing* the *same disavowal* that the *Makkans* will *hear* from the *Prophet*, and that the *hearing the same disavowal* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern* is *invariant*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *disavowal*, that the *prophet* is *not* *claiming* to be *above* the *idols*; he is *claiming* to be *free of them*, in the *sense of being* *the one who does not ascribe them to God*, in the *sense of being* *the one who does not participate in the ascription*, and that the *free of the ascription* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *mission* is *not a* *mission against the idols in the sense of attacking them*; it is *a mission of freedom from them*, and that the *freedom* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *conversion* of *verse 52* — the *turning to God* — is *not an* *addition of a new object*; it is a *freedom from the false objects*, in the way described in *verse 26's statement of the command as a subtraction*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:55

> **apart from Him. So scheme against me then, all together, and grant me no respite.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Apart from Him" — The Completion of the Disavowal**

The verse is the *completion* of the *disavowal* begun in *verse 54*: *ʿanhu* — "apart from Him." The *completion* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *disavowal* of *verse 54* is *not a* *general* *disavowal*; it is *a disavowal of the specific ascription*, in the *sense of being* *the disavowal of the partners that the ʿĀd ascribe apart from God*, in the *sense of being* *the disavowal that names the object of the freedom*. The sūrah is stating, in the completion, that the *prophet* is *not* *free of God*; he is *free of what is ascribed apart from God*, and that the *free of what is apart* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *tawḥīd* of *verse 50* — the *statement* that *there is no god other than Him* — is *the form of the disavowal*, in the *sense of being* *the disavowal that affirms the one and denies the many*, and that the *affirming and denying* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *position* is *not a* *negative* *position*; it is *the position of the one who affirms the one by the denial of the many*, in the way described in *verse 2's statement of the command as the whole of the message*.

**"Scheme Against Me Then, All Together" — The Invitation That the Sūrah Records**

The *second* *clause* of the *verse* is the *invitation*: *fa-mukīrūnī jamiʿan* — "scheme against me then, all together." The *invitation* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *afraid* of the *ʿĀd's* *power*; he is *inviting* them to *use it*, in the *sense of being* *the one who says to the strong: use your strength against me, for I am under the protection of the One who is stronger*, in the *sense of being* *the one who removes the fear from the equation*. The sūrah is stating, in the invitation, that the *prophet* is *not* *defending himself*; he is *challenging the ʿĀd to act*, and that the *challenging to act* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *in a position of weakness*; he is *in a position of confidence*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 33's statement of the prophet's answer to the challenge*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *invitation*, that the *ʿĀd's* *power* — the *power* they *believe* they *hold* — is *not* a *power* in the *sense of being* *able to harm the prophet*; it is a *power* in the *sense of being* *able only to harm themselves*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 8's statement of the mocked that will beset*.

**"And Grant Me No Respite" — The Word That Completes the Invitation**

The *verse* *closes* with the *word* that *completes* the *invitation*: *wa-lā taʾnī tharā* — "and grant me no respite." The *tharw* — respite, waiting — is the *word* that names the *delay* that the *ʿĀd* might *grant* the *prophet*, and the *refusal* of the *respite* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *asking* for *time*; he is *asking* them to *act now*, in the *sense of being* *the one who does not want the delay*, in the *sense of being* *the one who knows that the delay is not in his interest*. The sūrah is stating, in the closing, that the *prophet* is *not* *hesitating*; he is *urgent*, and that the *urgency* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *office* is *not an office of waiting*; it is an office of acting, in the way described in *verse 2's statement of the warner's function*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — *the prophet's statement of his trust* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *invitation* is *not a* *bravado*; it is *the statement of the one who has placed his trust in the One who is stronger than the schemers*, and that the *placed trust* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *tawakkul* — the *trust* — is *not a* *passive* *waiting*; it is *the active confidence of the one who is protected*, in the way described in *verse 41's statement of the name that moves the ark*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:56

> **Truly I trust in God, my Lord and your Lord. There is no creature that crawls, but that He holds it by its forelock. Truly my Lord is upon a straight path.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Truly I Trust in God, My Lord and Your Lord" — The Statement That Completes the Invitation**

Hūd's answer to his own invitation closes with the statement that is the verse's foundation: *innī tawakkaltu ʿalā-llāhi rabbi wa-rabbikum* — "truly I trust in God, my Lord and your Lord." The *tawakkul* — the trust — is the word that names the prophet's *relation* to the *schemers' power*, and the *relation* is *not* a *relation of equality*; it is a *relation of submission to the One who is above the schemers*, in the *sense of being* *the relation of the one who has placed his weight on the One who holds all weights*. The sūrah is stating, in the statement, that the *prophet* is *not* *meeting* the *schemers* *power* with *his own power*; he is *meeting* it with *the trust in the One who is their Lord as well*, and that the *meeting with the trust* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *alone* in the *confrontation*; he is *accompanied* by the *Lordship*, and that the *accompanied by the Lordship* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *schemers* are *not* *scheming* against the *prophet alone*; they are *scheming* against the *prophet and his Lord*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 30's statement of the prophet's question*.

**"My Lord and Your Lord" — The Phrase That Removes the Schemers from the Equation**

The *phrase* — *rabbi wa-rabbikum* — "my Lord and your Lord" — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* and the *schemers* are *not* in a *relation of two lords*; they are in a *relation of one Lord*, in the *sense of being* *the relation in which both are under the same Lordship*, in the *sense of being* *the relation in which the schemers' power is not their own but is held by the One who is also their Lord*. The sūrah is stating, in the phrase, that the *schemers* are *not* *above* the *prophet* by their *power*; they are *under* the *Lordship* that the *prophet* *trusts*, and that the *under the same Lordship* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *schemers'* *scheme* is *not a* *scheme against the prophet*; it is a *scheme under the Lordship*, and that the *under the Lordship* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *scheme* is *not* *free* in the *sense of being* *unconstrained*; it is *constrained* by the *Lordship*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 4's statement of the return to God*.

**"There Is No Creature That Crawls, but That He Holds It by Its Forelock" — The Image That the Sūrah Uses for the Lordship**

The *second* *clause* is the *image* that the *sūrah* uses for the *scope* of the *Lordship*: *mā min dābbatin illā ʿalayhi bi-nāḍīhā* — "there is no creature that crawls, but that He holds it by its forelock." The *nāḍī* — the forelock, the tuft of the forehead — is the *word* that names the *point* by which the *creature* is *held*, and the *image* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Lordship* is *not a* *distant* *sovereignty*; it is *a holding* in the *sense of being* *the act that holds each creature by the specific point of its life*, in the *sense of being* *the act that knows each creature at the point where it can be held*. The sūrah is stating, in the image, that the *Lordship* is *not* a *general* *fact*; it is a *specific* *act* for *each creature*, and that the *specific act* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *schemers* are *not* *outside* the *holding*; they are *held* by it, in the *way* *described* in *verse 6's statement of the provision of every crawling creature*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *image*, that the *schemers'* *power* is *not their own*; it is *held* by the *One* who *holds* them *by their forelock*, and that the *held by the forelock* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *scheme* is *not* a *free* *act*; it is a *held* *act*, and that the *held act* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *schemers* are *not* *scheming*; they are *being held while they think they are scheming*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 5's statement of the knowledge of what lies within breasts*.

**"Truly My Lord Is Upon a Straight Path" — The Closing That Names the State of the Lord**

The *verse* *closes* with the *statement* about the *Lord's* *state*: *wa-inna rabbi ʿalā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm* — "truly my Lord is upon a straight path." The *ṣirāṭ mustaqīm* — the straight path — is the *word* that the *Fātiḥah* *uses* for the *path* that the *believer* *asks God to guide him to* (1:6), and the *use* here, *applied to the Lord*, is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Lord* is *not* *upon a* *crooked* *path*; he is *upon the straight*, in the *sense of being* *the One whose way is not crooked*, in the *sense of being* *the One whose justice does not deviate*. The sūrah is stating, in the closing, that the *prophet's* *trust* is *not a* *trust in an uncertain Lord*; it is a *trust in the Lord whose path is straight*, and that the *trust in the straight* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *trusting blindly*; he is *trusting by knowledge of the Lord's state*, and that the *trusting by knowledge* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *tawakkul* is *not* a *suspension of the intellect*; it is a *placement of the intellect on the straight*, in the way described in *verse 23's statement of the humble before their Lord*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — the *schemers' response and the command* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *trust* is *not* *the end of the story*; it is the *ground* on which the *next* *words* are *spoken*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:57

> **But if you turn away, [know that] I have delivered unto you the message wherewith I have been sent by Him. My Lord will cause you to be succeeded by a people other than yourselves, and you harm Him not in the least. Truly my Lord is Watcher over all things."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"But If You Turn Away" — The Condition That the Sūrah States Without Expectation**

The sūrah now states the prophet's final word to the ʿĀd, and it states it under a condition that the prophet does not expect to be met: *fa-in taʿaadhtū* — "but if you turn away." The *condition* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* has *done* everything that the *office* requires, in the *sense of being* *the one who has called, denied the transaction, promised the rain, witnessed against the idols, invited the scheme, and placed his trust*, in the *sense of being* *the one who has no further word to give in the form of an argument*. The sūrah is stating, in the condition, that the *prophet* is *not* *despairing* of the *ʿĀd*; he is *stating the final form of the encounter*, in the *sense of being* *the form in which the messenger states what he has done and what will happen if the people do not turn*, and that the *stating the final form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *encounter* is *complete* in the *sense of being* *the encounter that has reached its end in the prophet's part*, in the way described in *verse 34's statement of the prophet's completion of his part*.

**"I Have Delivered Unto You the Message" — The Statement That Closes the Prophet's Account**

The *substance* of the *final word* is the *statement of delivery*: *innī balaḡtukum risālati rabbī ilaykum* — "I have delivered unto you the message wherewith I have been sent by Him." The *balagh* — the *delivery* — is the *word* that names the *prophet's* *function* in its *completed form*, and the *completed form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *responsible* for the *reception*; he is *responsible* for the *delivery*, and that the *responsible for the delivery* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *account* is *complete* when the *delivery* is *done*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 12's statement of the prophet as only a warner*. The sūrah is stating, in the statement, that the *ʿĀd's* *refusal* is *not* a *refusal that the prophet can answer*; it is a *refusal that the delivery has already met*, and that the *already met* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *burdened* with the *ʿĀd's* *fate*; he is *burdened* with the *message*, and the *message* is *delivered*.

**"My Lord Will Cause You to Be Succeeded by a People Other Than Yourselves" — The Promise of the Replacement**

The *second* *clause* is the *promise of the replacement*: *qalā yastakhlif rabbī qawman ghayrakum* — "my Lord will cause you to be succeeded by a people other than yourselves." The *istikhlāf* — the *replacement* — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ʿĀd's* *refusal* is *not* a *final* *event*; it is *the event that precedes the replacement*, in the *sense of being* *the event that makes the way clear for the next people*, in the *sense of being* *the event that the divine wisdom uses to move the human story forward*. The sūrah is stating, in the promise, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *the end of the story*; they are *a chapter*, and that the *chapter* is *closed* by the *replacement*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 48's statement of the communities that arise*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *promise*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *promise* *applied to the ʿĀd*, and that the *applied to the ʿĀd* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *not* *the final people*; they are *a chapter*, and that the *being a chapter* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *replacement* is *not a* *threat*; it is *a fact of the human story*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen*.

**"And You Harm Him Not in the Least" — The Statement That Removes the Schemers from the Divine Economy**

The *third* *clause* is the *statement* that *removes* the *schemers* from the *divine economy*: *wa-lā yaḍirrūnahū shayʾā* — "and you harm him not in the least." The *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ʿĀd's* *refusal* is *not* a *harm* to *God*; it is a *harm* to *themselves*, in the *sense of being* *the harm that they do to their own account*, in the *sense of being* *the harm that the replacement will make visible*. The sūrah is stating, in the statement, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *in a position to harm God* by their *refusal*, because *God did not create them out of need for them*, in the way described in *verse 6's statement of the provision held by God*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *statement*, that the *ʿĀd's* *scheme* of *verse 55* is *not a* *scheme that can reach God*; it is a *scheme that can only reach themselves*, and that the *only reach themselves* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *law of verse 8* — the *law* that *the mocked will beset* — is *the specific form* in which the *scheme returns to the schemers*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*.

**"Truly My Lord Is Watcher over All Things" — The Closing That the Sūrah Gives**

The *verse* *closes* with the *statement* that *governs* the *whole*: *wa-inna rabbi la-ḥafīẓun* — "truly my Lord is Watcher over all things." The *ḥafīẓ* — the *Watcher*, the *Guardian* — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *used* in *verse 12* for the *divine* *guardianship*, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *final word* is *not a* *new* *word*; it is *the same word* that the *sūrah* *opened* with, in the *sense of being* *the word of the guardianship that holds all things*, in the *sense of being* *the word that closes the encounter as it opened it*. The sūrah is stating, in the closing, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *outside* the *watching*; they are *within* it, in the *way* *described* in *verse 5's statement of the knowledge of the folded breast*, and that the *within the watching* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *encounter* is *not over* by the *prophet's* *final word*; it is *over* by the *command* that *will come* in *verse 58*, and that the *will come* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *next verse* is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *watching* is *not a* *passive* *act*; it is *the act that becomes the command*, in the way described in *verse 44's statement of the command that ends the flood*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:58

> **And when Our Command came, We saved Hūd and those who believed with him through a Mercy from Us; and We saved them from a grave punishment.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"And When Our Command Came" — The Command That the Sūrah States for the ʿĀd**

The sūrah states the coming of the command for the ʿĀd in the same form it stated for Noah in verse 40: *fa-idhā jāʾa amruna* — "and when Our command came." The *identity of form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* is *not a* *different kind of act* for *different peoples*; it is *the same act* in the *sense of being* *the act that comes at its appointed time*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the divine word effects upon the created order*. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *facing* a *different kind of punishment* from *Noah's people*; they are *facing the same kind*, in the *way* described in *verse 40's statement of the gushing forth*. The *commentators* *note* that the *command* for the *ʿĀd* was *in the form of a howling wind* (54:19), and that the *wind* is *the specific form* in which the *command* was *carried out*, in the *way* *described* in *46:24–25* — the *way* in which the *ʿĀd* *mistook* the *cloud* for *rain* and the *cloud* *proved* to be the *wind of the painful punishment*.

**The Mistaking of the Cloud — and the Sūrah's Way of Stating the Irony**

The *sūrah's* *statement* of the *command* carries, in the *commentators' gloss*, the *irony* that is *the verse's central note*: the *ʿĀd*, who had been *experiencing the drought* described in *verse 52*, *mistook* the *approaching cloud* — the *cloud* that they *expected* to bring the *rain* promised in *verse 52* — for a *sign of relief*, and the *cloud* *proved* to be the *wind*. The sūrah is stating, in the gloss, that the *ʿĀd's* *mistaking* is *not an accident*; it is *the form of their refusal*, in the *sense of being* *the refusal that makes the relief into the punishment*, in the *sense of being* *the refusal that cannot distinguish the mercy from the judgment because it has closed the senses described in verse 20*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *irony*, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *surprised* by the *wind*; they are *met* by the *wind* in the *form of the cloud they had hoped for*, and that the *met in the form of the hope* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *law of verse 8* — the *law* that *the mocked will beset* — is *the specific form* in which the *command* *besets*, in the *way* *described* throughout the *narrative middle*.

**"We Saved Hūd and Those Who Believed with Him through a Mercy from Us" — The Salvation, and the Mercy That Carries It**

The *second* *clause* states the *salvation*: *najjainā Hūdan wa-man āmana maʿahu bi-raḥmatin minnā* — "we saved Hūd and those who believed with him through a mercy from us." The *raḥmah* — the *mercy* — is the *word* that *governs* the *salvation*, and the *governing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *salvation* is *not a* *reward* in the *sense of being* *a payment for the belief*; it is a *mercy* in the *sense of being* *the act that goes beyond the account*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 41's statement of the forgiving and the merciful*. The sūrah is stating, in the clause, that the *believers* are *not* *saved by their own strength*; they are *saved by the mercy*, and that the *saved by the mercy* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *few* of *verse 40* — the *few* who *believed with Noah* — are *the same few* in *every story*, in the *sense of being* *the few that the mercy carries through the command*, in the *sense of being* *the few that the command does not reach*. The *commentators* *note* that those who *accepted Hūd's message* are *reported to have been forty thousand*, and that the *number* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *few* is *not a* *trivial* *number*; it is *the number of the saved*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 40's statement of the few who believed*.

**"And We Saved Them from a Grave Punishment" — The Closing That Names the Punishment**

The *verse* *closes* with the *statement* of the *punishment* from which the *believers* were *saved*: *wa-najjainā-hum min ʿadhābin qasiyyah* — "and we saved them from a grave punishment." The *qasiyyah* — grave, severe, painful — is the *word* that names the *quality* of the *punishment*, and the *quality* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *punishment* is *not a* *light* *event*; it is *the severe wind* that *uprooted the ʿĀd* in the *way* *described* in *54:20* — the *way* in which the *wind* *tore out the people as if they were uprooted palm trunks*. The sūrah is stating, in the closing, that the *salvation* of the *believers* is *not a* *salvation from a trivial event*; it is a *salvation from the grave*, and that the *salvation from the grave* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *belief* is *not a* *trivial* *act*; it is *the act that saves from the grave*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 23's statement of the inhabitants of the Garden*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — the *characterization* of the *ʿĀd* and the *refrain* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *story* is *not over* by the *salvation*; it is *closed* by the *verdict*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 44's statement of the refrain*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:59

> **That was ʿĀd. They rejected the signs of their Lord, disobeyed the messengers, and followed the command of every stubborn tyrant.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"That Was ʿĀd" — The Sūrah's Statement of the Completed Story**

The sūrah now steps out of the narrative to state, in its own voice, the *characterization* of the *completed story*: *dhālika ʿĀd* — "that was ʿĀd." The *dhālika* — "that" — is the *word* that *points* to the *story* as a *whole*, and the *pointing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *story* is *not a* *series of events*; it is *a case*, in the *sense of being* *the case that the sūrah has stated in full*, in the *sense of being* *the case that the characterization will summarize*. The sūrah is stating, in the pointing, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *a people in the distance*; they are *a case before the reader*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *pointing*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *pointing* *applied to the ʿĀd*, and that the *applied to the ʿĀd* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same case*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*.

**"They Rejected the Signs of Their Lord" — The First Mark of the Characterization**

The *first* *mark* of the *characterization* is the *rejection of the signs*: *kafarū bi-āyāti rabbi-him* — "they rejected the signs of their Lord." The *āyāt* — the *signs* — is the *word* that the *Qur'an* uses for the *evidence* of the *divine* *act*, and the *rejection* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *without* the *signs*; they are *with* the *signs* and *against* them, in the *sense of being* *the ones who see the signs and refuse them*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who have the evidence present and do not engage it*. The sūrah is stating, in the mark, that the *ʿĀd's* *refusal* is *not a* *refusal of the absent*; it is a *refusal of the present*, in the way described in *verse 28's statement of the blindness to the clear proof*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *mark*, that the *Makkans'* *refusal* is *the same refusal*, and that the *same refusal* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *case* is *not over*; it is *in operation* in the *Makkans*, in the way described in *verse 5's statement of the folded breast*.

**"Disobeyed the Messengers" — The Second Mark, and the Word That Includes the Prophet**

The *second* *mark* is the *disobedience of the messengers*: *wa-ʿaaw al-rusul* — "they disobeyed the messengers." The *plural* — *al-rusul* — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *disobedience* is *not a* *disobedience of a single messenger*; it is a *disobedience of the office*, in the *sense of being* *the disobedience that targets the prophetic function rather than the individual prophet*, in the *sense of being* *the disobedience that the ʿĀd directed at Hūd as the representative of the office*. The sūrah is stating, in the plural, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *refusing Hūd as a person*; they are *refusing the office he carries*, and that the *refusing the office* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *refusal of the Prophet* is *a refusal of the office*, in the way described in *verse 12's statement of the prophet as only a warner*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *mark*, that the *disobedience* is *not a* *failure of the messenger*; it is *a choice of the disobeider*, and that the *choice* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *just*, in the way described in *verse 34's statement of the prophet's completion of his part*.

**"Followed the Command of Every Stubborn Tyrant" — The Third Mark, and the Word That Names the Social Structure**

The *third* *mark* is the *following of the stubborn tyrant*: *wa-attabaʿū amra kulli jawvā ʿattīd* — "they followed the command of every stubborn tyrant." The *jawvā ʿattīd* — the *stubborn tyrant* — is the *word* that names the *social structure* that the *ʿĀd* *followed*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ʿĀd's* *refusal* is *not a* *refusal of individuals*; it is a *refusal of the structure*, in the *sense of being* *the refusal that follows the tyrant rather than the truth*, in the *sense of being* *the refusal that makes the tyrant's command the measure of the right*. The sūrah is stating, in the mark, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *refusing the message because it is false*; they are *refusing it because the tyrant refuses it*, and that the *refusing because the tyrant refuses* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *social structure* is *the form of the refusal*, in the way described in *verse 27's statement of the notables' social epistemology*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *mark*, that the *Makkans'* *notables* are *the stubborn tyrants* of the *Makkans'* *case*, and that the *being the tyrants* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *structure* is *not over*; it is *in operation* in the *Makkans*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*, and that the *in operation* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *next verse* — the *curse* and the *refrain* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *case* is *closed* by the *verdict*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:60

> **And they were pursued by a curse in this world, and [shall be] on the Day of Resurrection. Behold! Truly ʿĀd disbelieved in their Lord. Behold! Away with ʿĀd, the people of Hūd!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Pursued by a Curse in This World" — The First Half of the Double Verdict**

The sūrah closes the ʿĀd story with a *double* *verdict*: *wa-laḥiqat-humu-l-laʿnatu fī-d-dunyā wa- yawma-l-qiyāmah* — "they were pursued by a curse in this world, and [shall be] on the Day of Resurrection." The *double* *verdict* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ʿĀd's* *fate* is *not a* *single* *event*; it is *a fate in two times*, in the *sense of being* *the fate that is pursued in this world and in the next*, in the *sense of being* *the fate that does not end at the wind*. The *commentators* *note* that the *curse* is *the distancing from God's mercy*, and that the *distancing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *punished* in the *sense of being* *struck*; they are *distanced* in the *sense of being* *separated from the source of the good*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 18's statement of the curse as exile from the mercy*. The sūrah is stating, in the double verdict, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *finished* with the *curse* by the *wind*; the *wind* is *the form of the curse in this world*, and the *Day of Resurrection* is *the form of the curse in the next*, and that the *two forms* together form the *sūrah's* complete *description* of the *fate*, in the way described in *verse 39's pairing of the disgrace and the enduring*.

**"Truly ʿĀd Disbelieved in Their Lord" — The Reason That the Sūrah States**

The *second* *clause* states the *reason* for the *double* *verdict*: *inna ʿĀdan kafarū rabba-hum* — "truly ʿĀd disbelieved in their Lord." The *reason* is *not a* *list of sins*; it is a *single* *statement* of the *disbelief in the Lord*, and the *single statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ʿĀd's* *entire* *refusal* — the *refusal of the signs*, the *disobedience of the messengers*, the *following of the tyrant* — is *one act* in the *sense of being* *the act of the disbelief in the Lord*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the other acts are forms of*. The sūrah is stating, in the reason, that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *punished for three sins*; they are *punished for one*, and that the *one* is *the disbelief in the Lord*, in the way described in *verse 50's statement of the people as fabricators*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *reason*, that the *Makkans'* *refusal* is *the same one refusal*, and that the *same one refusal* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *case* is *not over*; it is *in operation* in the *Makkans*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*.

**"Away with ʿĀd, the People of Hūd!" — The Second Strike of the Refrain**

The *verse* *closes* with the *second* *strike* of the *refrain*: *wailan li-ʿĀdin jamīʿan* — "away with ʿĀd, the people of Hūd!" The *strike* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *a people in the distance*; they are *a people done away*, in the *sense of being* *the people that the verdict has spoken over*, in the *sense of being* *the people that the refrain has marked*. The sūrah is stating, in the strike, that the *ʿĀd* are *named* in the *refrain* as *the people of Hūd*, and that the *named as the people of Hūd* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ʿĀd* are *not* *a people without a prophet*; they are *a people with a prophet that they refused*, and that the *with a prophet that they refused* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *case* is *the case of the prophet refused*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *strike*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *second* *strike* *applied to the ʿĀd*, and that the *applied to the ʿĀd* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same case*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next story* — *the story of Ṣāliḥ* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern* is *not over*; it is *repeating* in the *Thamūd*, in the way described in *verse 48's statement of the communities for whom enjoyment is granted*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:61

> **And to Thamūd, their brother Ṣāliḥ. He said, "O my people! Worship God! You have no god other than Him. He brought you forth from the earth and settled you therein. So seek forgiveness from Him; then turn unto Him in repentance. Truly my Lord is Nigh, Responsive."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"And to Thamūd, Their Brother Ṣāliḥ" — The Third Story, and the Word That Opens It**

The sūrah opens the third story of its narrative middle with the same form it used for ʿĀd: *wa-ilā Thamūdin akhāhum Ṣāliḥ* — "and to Thamūd, their brother Ṣāli." The *identity of form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *stories* are *not* *different kinds of events*; they are *the same kind* in the *sense of being* *the same pattern of the brother sent to the people*, in the *sense of being* *the same pattern of the call and the refusal and the command*. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *a different case* from the *ʿĀd*; they are *the same case* in the *sense of being* *the case of the brother refused*, in the way described in *verse 50's statement of the pattern*. The *commentators* *note* that *Ṣāliḥ* was *an Arabian prophet* not mentioned in the *Bible*, and that the *Thamūd* *inhabited* a *rocky plain* in *western Arabia* between the *Ḥijāz* and *Syria*, and that they were *descendants of Noah* in the *way* the *ʿĀd* were, and that the *descendants of Noah* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *nations* of the *narrative middle* are *not* *separate peoples*; they are *one people divided by time*, in the way described in *verse 48's statement of the communities that arise from those with Noah*.

**"He Brought You Forth from the Earth and Settled You Therein" — The Argument from the Origin**

Ṣāliḥ's call to his people includes an *argument* that the calls of *Noah* and *Hūd* did not state in the same form: *khalaqakum min al-arḍi wa-stakharakum fīhā* — "he brought you forth from the earth and settled you therein." The *argument* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *call* is *not* a *series of commands*; it is *a call with a ground*, in the *sense of being* *the call that states the relation of the people to the Lord in the form of their origin*, in the *sense of being* *the call that reminds the people that they are not self-originated*. The sūrah is stating, in the argument, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *a people without an origin*; they are *a people brought forth from the earth*, and that the *brought forth* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd's* *claim to self-sufficiency* — the *claim* that the *ʿĀd* made in *their* *refusal* — is *not a* *claim that the earth supports*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 6's statement of the provision held by God*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *argument*, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *settled in the earth by their own power*; they are *settled by the Lord*, and that the *settled by the Lord* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd's* *use of the earth* is *not their own right*; it is *a trust held by the Lord*, in the way described in *verse 52's statement of the strength that God adds*.

**"Seek Forgiveness from Him; Then Turn Unto Him in Repentance" — The Practical Form, Repeated**

The *second* *part* of the *call* is the *practical form* that the *sūrah* has *stated* in *verse 3* and *verse 52*: *istaghfirūhu thumma ūbū ilayhi* — "seek forgiveness from him; then turn unto him in repentance." The *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *practical form* is *not a* *variable*; it is *the constant* of the *call*, in the *sense of being* *the form that every prophet gives when he calls his people to the conversion*, in the *sense of being* *the form that the Makkans are being asked to perform now and that the Thamūd were asked to perform then*. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* their *own call in its practical form* *spoken to a third people*, and that the *hearing in the practical form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *call* is *not a theory*; it is a *practice*, in the way described in *verse 52's statement of the practical form of the call*.

**"Truly My Lord Is Nigh, Responsive" — The Closing That Names the Two Attributes**

The *verse* *closes* with the *naming* of the *two* *attributes* that *govern* the *call*: *innī rabbi qarībun mujīb* — "truly my Lord is Nigh, Responsive." The *qurb* — the *nearness* — and the *ijābah* — the *response* — are the *two attributes* that the *sūrah* *names* for the *Lord* in this *verse*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Lord* is *not* *distant* from the *supplicant*; he is *nigh*, in the *sense of being* *the One who is close to the one who calls*, in the *sense of being* *the One who answers the call when it is made*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *calling into a distance*; they are *calling into a nearness*, and that the *calling into the nearness* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *duʿāʾ* — the *supplication* — is *not a* *cry into the void*; it is *a cry into the nearness that answers*, in the way described in *2:186* — the *way* in which God states: "When My servants ask thee about Me, truly I am near. I answer the call of the caller when he calls Me." The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — the *Thamūd's* *response* and the *she-camel* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *nearness* is *not* *the end of the story*; it is *the ground* on which the *next* *words* are *spoken*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:62

> **They said, "O Ṣāli! You were a source of hope among us before this. Do you forbid us to worship that which our fathers worshipped, while we remain in grave doubt about that to which you call us?"**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"You Were a Source of Hope Among Us Before This" — The Acknowledgment That the Sūrah Records**

The Thamūd's response to Ṣāliḥ's call opens with an *acknowledgment* that the sūrah records with particular care: *qālū yā Ṣāliḥ qad kunta fi-nā ma-rjāan qabla hādha* — "you were a source of hope among us before this." The *marjāʾ* — the *source of hope*, the *one looked to* — is the *word* that names the *position* that *Ṣāliḥ* *held* in the *Thamūd's* *society* before the *call*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that *Ṣāliḥ* was *not* an *outsider* to the *Thamūd*; he was *one of their most noble and respected figures*, in the *sense of being* *the one they looked to for leadership*, in the *sense of being* *the one whose word carried weight in their assembly*. The sūrah is stating, in the recording, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *refusing an unknown*; they are *refusing the one they had hoped in*, and that the *refusing the one they had hoped in* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *refusal* is *not a* *refusal of the foreign*; it is *a refusal of the domestic*, in the way described in *Noah's address of verse 28*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *acknowledgment*, that the *Thamūd's* *refusal* is *more* *grave* than the *refusal of an outsider*, because it is *the refusal of the one who was the hope*, and that the *more grave* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *case* is *not the case of the stranger*; it is *the case of the hope betrayed*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*.

**"Do You Forbid Us to Worship That Which Our Fathers Worshipped" — The Appeal to the Fathers, Named**

The *second* *clause* of the *response* is the *appeal* that the *sūrah* will *hear* *repeated* in *several* *stories*: *a-taḥarramnā an naʿbuda mā ʿabada ābāʾunā* — "do you forbid us to worship that which our fathers worshipped?" The *appeal* to the *fathers* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd's* *test* for the *legitimacy of the worship* is *not the content of the worship*; it is *the history of the worship*, in the *sense of being* *the test that measures the new by the old*, in the *sense of being* *the test that requires the new to be continuous with the old to be legitimate*. The sūrah is stating, in the recording, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *engaging with the argument of verse 61* — the *argument from the origin* — but with the *authority of the fathers*, and that the *engaging with the authority of the fathers* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd* are *in* the *state* of *verse 109* — the *state* of *worshipping only as the fathers worshipped* — and that the *in the state* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *appeal to the fathers* is *the same appeal*, in the way described in *2:170* — the *way* in which the *Qur'an* answers the *appeal*: "What! Even though their fathers understood nothing, and were not rightly guided?" The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *appeal to the fathers* is *not an argument*; it is a *refusal to argue*, in the sense of being *the refusal that places the fathers' worship above the question of its truth*, and that the *refusal to argue* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *just*, in the way described in *verse 34's statement of the prophet's completion of his part*.

**"While We Remain in Grave Doubt About That to Which You Call Us" — The Doubt, Stated as a Position**

The *response* *closes* with the *statement of the doubt*: *wa-inna-nā fī mā taʿūduna ilayhi la-fī hakatun balīḥah* — "while we remain in grave doubt about that to which you call us." The *hakat* — the *doubt*, the *perplexity* — is the *word* that names the *Thamūd's* *state* toward the *call*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd* are *not* *without* a *response* to the *call*; they are *with* a *response*, and the *response* is *the doubt*, in the *sense of being* *the state of the one who is perplexed by the call*, in the *sense of being* *the state of the one who has not resolved the call into acceptance or refusal*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Thamūd's* *doubt* is *not a* *doubt that will be resolved by more evidence*; it is *a doubt that is the form of the refusal*, in the way described in *verse 20's statement of the spiritual deafness and blindness*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *open* to the *call*; they are *closed* in the *form of the doubt*, and that the *closed in the form of the doubt* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *next verse* — *Ṣāliḥ's* *answer* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not finished* with the *people*; he is *turning to the clear proof*, in the way described in *verse 28's statement of the burhān*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:63

> **He said, "O my people! What think you: if I stand upon a clear proof from my Lord, and He has granted me Mercy from Himself, then who can help me against God, were I to disobey Him? For you would increase me in naught but loss."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"What Think You: If I Stand Upon a Clear Proof" — The Rhetorical Question, Repeated in Its Invariant Form**

Ṣāliḥ's answer to the Thamūd's doubt is the *same* *rhetorical question* that *Noah* asked in *verse 28*: *yā qawmī afa-ḍālltumum in kuntu ʿalā-burhānin min rabbi* — "O my people! what think you: if I stand upon a clear proof from my Lord." The *identity* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *question* is *not a* *prophet's* *individual* *formulation*; it is *the form of the prophetic presentation itself*, in the *sense of being* *the form that every prophet takes when he presents the burhān to his doubting people*, in the *sense of being* *the form that places the question in the people's hands*. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *Thamūd* are *hearing* the *same question* that *Noah's people* *heard*, and that the *hearing the same question* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern* is *invariant*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Thamūd's* *doubt* of *verse 62* is *not met by a new argument*; it is *met by the same presentation*, and that the *met by the same presentation* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *burhān* is *not a* *variable* *evidence*; it is *the constant* of the *prophetic office*, in the way described in *verse 17's statement of the clear proof*.

**"He Has Granted Me Mercy from Himself" — The Warrant, Repeated**

The *second* *clause* is the *warrant* that *Noah* *stated* in *verse 28*: *wa-wahaba lī minhu raḥmah* — "and He has granted me mercy from Himself." The *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *warrant* is *not a* *prophet's* *individual* *claim*; it is *the standing warrant of the prophetic office*, in the *sense of being* *the warrant that every prophet states when he presents the burhān*, in the *sense of being* *the warrant that places the source of the burhān in the divine mercy*. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Ṣāliḥ's* *claim to prophethood* is *not a* *claim to personal merit*; it is a *claim to the mercy*, in the way described in *verse 51's statement of the reward that lies only with the One who originated the prophet*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *being asked* to *trust* in *Ṣāliḥ's* *merit*; they are *being asked* to *trust* in the *mercy that granted him the burhān*, and that the *being asked to trust in the mercy* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *call* is *not a* *call to a person*; it is *a call to the mercy that sent the person*, in the way described in *verse 2's statement of the warner on God's behalf*.

**"Who Can Help Me Against God, Were I to Disobey Him?" — The Question That Inverts the Frame**

The *central* *clause* is the *question* that *Noah* *asked* in *verse 30*: *fa-man yanṣurunī min Allāh in ʿaṣaytuhū* — "who can help me against God, were I to disobey him?" The *question* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *afraid* of the *Thamūd's* *power*; he is *stating* that his *accountability* is *not to the people*; it is to the *Lord*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 30's statement of the question that inverts the frame*. The sūrah is stating, in the recording, that the *Thamūd's* *doubt* is *not met by a defense of the prophet*; it is *met by the statement of the prophet's accountability to the Lord*, and that the *met by the statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *position* is *not a* *position that the Thamūd can threaten*, in the way described in *verse 56's statement of the trust in the Lord*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *in a position to demand* from *Ṣāliḥ* a *different form of the call*; they are *in a position to receive the call or refuse it*, and that the *in a position to receive or refuse* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *just*, in the way described in *verse 34's statement of the prophet's completion of his part*.

**"For You Would Increase Me in Naught but Loss" — The Closing That Names the Consequence**

The *verse* *closes* with the *statement of the consequence*: *inna-kum lā tazīdūnī illā ḍalālā* — "for you would increase me in naught but loss." The *ḍalāl* — the *loss*, the *misguidance* — is the *word* that names the *state* that the *Thamūd's* *doubt* would *produce in the prophet* if he *yielded to it*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *immune* to the *effect of the doubt*; he is *the one who states that the doubt would increase him in loss*, in the *sense of being* *the one who knows that yielding to the doubt would be a misguidance for him*, in the *sense of being* *the one who states the consequence of the yielding*. The sūrah is stating, in the closing, that the *prophet* is *not* *refusing the Thamūd's doubt out of pride*; he is *refusing it out of knowledge of its consequence*, and that the *refusing out of knowledge* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *firmness* is *not a* *weakness of feeling*; it is *a strength of knowing*, in the way described in *verse 56's statement of the trust placed on the straight*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — the *she-camel* and the *hamstringing* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd* are *not* *persuaded* by the *question*; they are *demanding a sign*, in the way described in *verse 32's statement of the notables' challenge*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:64

> **O my people! This she-camel of God is a sign unto you. Leave her to graze freely on God's earth, and cause her no harm, lest you be seized by a punishment nigh."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"This She-Camel of God" — The Sign That the Sūrah Names**

The sūrah now states the *sign* that the *Thamūd* had *demanded* and *Ṣāliḥ* *was given*: *hādhihi naʿamat Allāhi lakum āyah* — "this she-camel of God is a sign unto you." The *nāʿamah* — the *she-camel* — is the *word* that names the *sign*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *sign* is *not a* *mystical* *event*; it is *a creature* in the *sense of being* *the camel that the Thamūd could see and touch and hear*, in the *sense of being* *the sign that is present in the created order*. The *commentators* *note* that *Ṣāliḥ* *brought* the *she-camel* *forth from a large rock* in *accordance with the Thamūd's request for a miracle*, and that the *brought forth from the rock* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *sign* is *not a* *natural* *birth*; it is *a divine act* in the *sense of being* *the act that produces the camel from the stone*, in the *way* *described* in *46:24* and *54:27*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *faced with an abstract proof*; they are *faced with a creature*, and that the *faced with a creature* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *sign* is *not a* *theory*; it is *a presence*, in the way described in *verse 40's statement of the command that produces the ark*.

**"A Sign Unto You" — and the Two Parts of the Command**

The *sūrah* *states* that the *she-camel* is *a sign*, and the *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *sign* is *not a* *reward*; it is *a test*, in the *sense of being* *the event that the Thamūd are to receive with obedience*, in the *sense of being* *the event that will be the measure of their response*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *naming of the sign*, that the *command* connected to the *sign* has *two parts*: the *part* stated in *this verse* — *the command to let the camel graze freely and cause her no harm* — and the *part* stated in *26:155* — *the command that the camel shall have drink on her appointed day and the people on theirs*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *two parts*, that the *sign* is *not a* *one-sided* *gift*; it is *a relation* in the *sense of being* *the relation that the Thamūd are to hold with the camel*, in the *sense of being* *the relation of the shared earth and the shared water*, and that the *relation* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *camel* is *not a* *property of the Thamūd*; she is *a sign held in trust*, in the way described in *verse 61's statement of the settlement in the earth as a trust*.

**"Leave Her to Graze Freely on God's Earth" — The Command That Places the Camel in the Divine Economy**

The *first* *part* of the *command* is the *command to let the camel graze*: *dharuhā taʿmalu fī-arḍi-llāh* — "leave her to graze freely on God's earth." The *ʿamal* — the *grazing*, the *movement upon the earth* — is the *word* that names the *camel's* *right* to the *earth*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *earth* is *not the Thamūd's property*; it is *God's earth*, in the *sense of being* *the earth that the Lord holds and allocates*, in the *sense of being* *the earth that the camel has a right to by the Lord's allocation*. The sūrah is stating, in the command, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *the owners of the earth*; they are *the settlers of verse 61*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who are settled in the earth by the Lord*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who hold the earth in trust*, and that the *holding in trust* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd's* *use of the earth* is *not their own right*; it is *a right held by the Lord and shared with the camel*, in the way described in *verse 6's statement of the provision held by God for every creature*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *command*, that the *Thamūd* who *diminish the measure and the balance* — the *Thamūd* who *defraud in their business relations*, in the *way* *described* in *the story of Shuʿayb* that follows — are *not* *a people who can hold the earth in trust*, and that the *not able to hold* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *camel* is *the test of the trust*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 7's statement of the trial as to which is best in deed*.

**"And Cause Her No Harm, Lest You Be Seized by a Punishment Nigh" — The Warning That Closes the Command**

The *second* *part* of the *command* is the *warning*: *wa-lā tamsassūhā bi-sarrin fa-yaḫidhahū ʿadhāb qarīb* — "and cause her no harm, lest you be seized by a punishment nigh." The *qarīb* — the *nigh* — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *used* in *verse 39* for the *proximity of the account*, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *punishment* is *not a* *distant* *threat*; it is *nigh* in the *sense of being* *within the time of the Thamūd*, in the *sense of being* *the punishment that the hamstringing will bring*. The sūrah is stating, in the warning, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *being warned of a general evil*; they are *being warned of the specific punishment that the harming of the camel will bring*, and that the *being warned of the specific* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* is *not a* *general exhortation*; it is *a specific command with a specific consequence*, in the way described in *verse 39's pairing of the two punishments*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *hamstringing* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd* are *not* *obedient* to the *command*, and that the *not obedient* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern* of the *narrative middle* is *repeating* in the *Thamūd*, in the way described throughout the *stories*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:65

> **But they hamstrung her. So he said, "Enjoy yourselves in your abode for three days—that is a promise that will not be belied."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"But They Hamstrung Her" — The Act That the Sūrah States in a Single Word**

The sūrah states the Thamūd's act against the she-camel in a single word: *fa-ʿaqarūhā* — "but they hamstrung her." The *ʿaqr* — the *hamstringing* — is the *word* that names the *specific act* of *cutting the camel's sinews* to *render her unable to stand or flee*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd's* *act* is *not a* *mistake*; it is *a deliberate act* in the *sense of being* *the act that requires the knowledge of the camel's anatomy*, in the *sense of being* *the act that requires the intention to disable*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *accidentally harming the camel*; they are *choosing to disable the sign*, and that the *choosing to disable* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *act* is *not a* *failure of care*; it is *a defiance of the command*, in the way described in *7:77* — the *way* in which the *Thamūd* are *described* as *insolently defying the command of their Lord*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *naming*, that the *Makkans* who *harm the sign* — the *Makkans* who *mock the Prophet and the believers* — are *in the same act*, in the *way* *described* throughout the *narrative middle*.

**"Enjoy Yourselves in Your Abode for Three Days" — The Word That the Sūrah Records**

Ṣāliḥ's answer to the hamstringing is the *word* that the sūrah records with particular solemnity: *qāla taʾattamū dārakum thalāthata ayyām* — "enjoy yourselves in your abode for three days." The *word* is *not a* *threat* in the *sense of being* *a demand for reparation*; it is *a statement of the term*, in the *sense of being* *the statement of the time that remains to the Thamūd before the command comes*, in the *sense of being* *the word that gives the Thamūd the knowledge of their remaining time*. The sūrah is stating, in the recording, that the *Ṣāliḥ* is *not* *asking the Thamūd to repent*; he is *stating the term*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 3's statement of the appointed term*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *three days* are *not a* *gift of time*; they are *the form of the term that the command holds*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 8's statement of the muddat* — the *limited period of delay*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *word*, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *being given a chance to avoid the punishment by the three days*; they are *being given the knowledge of the punishment's arrival*, and that the *being given the knowledge* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *just*, because the *Thamūd* *know* the time, in the way described in *verse 34's statement of the prophet's completion of his part*.

**"That Is a Promise That Will Not Be Belied" — The Warrant That Closes the Word**

The *verse* *closes* with the *warrant*: *wa-huwa ʿaduwwun lā yukhaḍab* — "that is a promise that will not be belied." The *ʿadhw* — the *promise*, the *term* — is the *word* that names the *divine commitment* to the *time*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *three days* are *not a* *prediction*; they are *a promise* in the *sense of being* *the commitment that the Lord makes to the time*, in the *sense of being* *the term that the command holds and will execute*. The sūrah is stating, in the closing, that the *Ṣāliḥ* is *not* *stating a guess*; he is *stating the promise*, and that the *stating the promise* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's word* is *not a* *word of his own*; it is *the word of the promise*, in the way described in *verse 1's statement of the Book that is determined and expounded from the Presence of the Wise and Aware*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *coming of the command* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *promise* is *not belied*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 58's statement of the command that comes for the ʿĀd*, and that the *not belied* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *promise* *stated to the Thamūd*, and that the *stated to the Thamūd* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same case*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:66

> **So when Our Command came, We saved Ṣāliḥ and those who believed with him, through a Mercy from Us, and from the disgrace of that day. Truly thy Lord, He is the Strong, the Mighty.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"So When Our Command Came" — The Promise Kept in Its Form**

The sūrah states the coming of the command for the Thamūd in the same form it stated for Noah in verse 40 and for the ʿĀd in verse 58: *fa-idhā jāʾa amruna* — "so when Our command came." The *identity of form across the three stories* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* is *not a* *different act* for *different peoples*; it is *the same act* in the *sense of being* *the act that comes at the appointed time*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the promise of verse 65 holds and executes*. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *promise* is *not belied*, in the way stated in *verse 65's closing*, and that the *not belied* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *divine word* is *not a* *word that can be delayed or altered*, in the way described in *verse 1's statement of the determined Book*. The *commentators* *note* that the *command* for the *Thamūd* was *in the form of a terrible loud sound accompanied by an earthquake*, and that the *sound* is *the specific form* in which the *command* was *carried out*, in the *way* *described* in *7:78* — the *way* in which the *Cry* *seized* the *Thamūd* and *morning found them lying lifeless in their abode*.

**"We Saved Ṣāliḥ and Those Who Believed with Him through a Mercy from Us" — The Salvation, Repeated in Its Invariant Form**

The *second* *clause* states the *salvation* in the *same form* stated for *Noah* in *verse 40* and for *Hūd* in *verse 58*: *najjainā Ṣāliḥan wa-man āmana maʿahu bi-raḥmatin minnā* — "we saved Ṣāliḥ and those who believed with him through a mercy from us." The *identity of form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *salvation* is *not a* *different kind of act* for *different prophets*; it is *the same act* in the *sense of being* *the act of the mercy that carries the believers through the command*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the command does not reach*. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *believers of the Thamūd* are *not* *a different kind of few* from the *believers of Noah and the ʿĀd*; they are *the same few* in the *way* *described* in *verse 40's statement of the few who believed*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans'* *believers* — the *few* of *their own time* — are *the same few*, and that the *same few* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *salvation* is *not a* *past event*; it is *the form of the salvation that the Makkans are being offered now*, in the way described in *verse 23's statement of the inhabitants of the Garden*.

**"And from the Disgrace of That Day" — The Word That the Sūrah Adds**

The *third* *clause* adds a *word* that the *previous statements of salvation* did not include: *wa-min kharāḍi-yawm-in dhālika* — "and from the disgrace of that day." The *kharādh* — the *disgrace*, the *humiliation* — is the *word* that names the *quality* of the *day* from which the *believers* were *saved*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *day* is *not a* *neutral* *event*; it is *the day of the disgrace* in the *sense of being* *the day on which the Thamūd were humiliated by the command*, in the *sense of being* *the day that the mockery of verse 38 is fulfilled in*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *believers* are *not* *saved from a trivial event*; they are *saved from the disgrace*, and that the *saved from the disgrace* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *belief* is *not a* *trivial act*; it is *the act that saves from the disgrace*, in the way described in *verse 39's pairing of the punishment that disgraces and the punishment that endures*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans'* *believers* are *the ones who will be saved from the disgrace of their own day*, and that the *saved from the disgrace* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *promise of verse 49* — the *promise that the end belongs to the reverent* — is *the specific promise that this verse demonstrates*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*.

**"Truly Thy Lord, He Is the Strong, the Mighty" — The Closing That Names the Two Attributes**

The *verse* *closes* with the *naming* of the *two* *attributes* that *govern* the *command* and the *salvation*: *innā rabbaka huwa-l-qawiyyu-l-ʿazīz* — "truly thy Lord, He is the Strong, the Mighty." The *qwah* — the *strength* — and the *ʿiẓah* — the *might* — are the *two attributes* that the *sūrah* *names* for the *Lord* in this *closing*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* is *not a* *weak* *act*; it is *the act of the Strong* in the *sense of being* *the act that cannot be resisted*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the creation does not withstand*, and that the *salvation* is *not a* *trivial* *act*; it is *the act of the Mighty* in the *sense of being* *the act that the command cannot reach*. The sūrah is stating, in the closing, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *naming of the attributes* *applied to their Lord*, and that the *applied to their Lord* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *refusal* is *not a* *refusal that can withstand the Strong and the Mighty*, in the way described in *verse 4's statement of the One who is Powerful over all things*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — the *Cry* and the *refrain* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *strength and the might* are *not abstract attributes*; they are *the act that the Cry carries out*, in the way described in *verse 67*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:67

> **The Cry seized those who did wrong, and morning found them lying lifeless in their abode,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"The Cry Seized Those Who Did Wrong" — The Form of the Command, Named**

The sūrah now states the *specific form* in which the *command* *seized* the *Thamūd*: *fa-akhadha-l-ladhīna ẓalamū aṣ-ṣayḥah* — "the Cry seized those who did wrong." The *ṣayḥah* — the *Cry*, the *terrible loud sound* — is the *word* that names the *form* of the *command* for the *Thamūd*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* is *not a* *silent* *act*; it is *the act that the Cry carries*, in the *sense of being* *the sound that the earth produces when the command passes through it*, in the *sense of being* *the sound that the Thamūd hear as the last sound of their world*. The *commentators* *note* that the *punishment* was *an earthquake accompanied by the terrible loud sound*, and that the *earthquake and the sound* together form the *Cry*, in the *way* *described* in *7:78*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *destroyed in silence*; they are *seized by the sound*, and that the *seized by the sound* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* is *not a* *hidden* *act*; it is *the act that the creation hears*, in the way described in *verse 58's statement of the howling wind for the ʿĀd*.

**"Those Who Did Wrong" — The Word That the Sūrah Uses for the Seized**

The *sūrah* *names* the *seized* as *alladhīna ẓalamū* — "those who did wrong," and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Cry* is *not a* *Cry for all the Thamūd*; it is *the Cry for the wrongdoers*, in the *sense of being* *the sound that seizes the ones who hamstrung the camel*, in the *sense of being* *the sound that does not reach the believers saved in verse 66*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *command* is *not a* *general* *act*; it is *the act that distinguishes*, in the way described in *verse 40's statement of the exception in the family of Noah*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *naming*, that the *Makkans* who *do wrong* — the *Makkans* who *harm the sign and mock the prophet* — are *the ones whom the Cry will seize*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 20's statement of the multiplied punishment*.

**"And Morning Found Them Lying Lifeless in Their Abode" — The Image That the Sūrah Gives**

The *second* *clause* of the *verse* is the *image* that the *sūrah* gives of the *state* of the *seized*: *wa-ṣabaḥū fī dāri-him jāmidīn* — "and morning found them lying lifeless in their abode." The *jāmid* — the *lifeless*, the *stiff*, the *frozen* — is the *word* that names the *state* of the *Thamūd* at *morning*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Cry* is *not a* *Cry that leaves the Thamūd in a state of struggle*; it is *the Cry that leaves them lifeless*, in the *sense of being* *the sound that stops the life in the moment it passes*, in the *sense of being* *the sound that the morning finds completed*. The sūrah is stating, in the image, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *destroyed in a process*; they are *found*, in the *sense of being* *the ones that the morning discovers in the state of the lifeless*, in the *sense of being* *the ones whose destruction is complete before the morning rises*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *image*, that the *command* is *not a* *slow* *act*; it is *the act that the morning finds done*, in the way described in *verse 44's statement of the water that recedes and the command that is carried out*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing of the verse*, that the *next verse* — the *as though they had never dwelt there* and the *refrain* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *state of the lifeless* is *not the end of the description*; it is *the state that the refrain will complete*, in the way described in *verse 44's statement of the refrain struck over the wrongdoing people*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:68

> **as though they had never dwelt there. Behold! Truly Thamūd disbelieved in their Lord. Behold! Away with Thamūd!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"As Though They Had Never Dwelt There" — The Image of the Complete Erasure**

The sūrah completes the description of the Thamūd's state with the image that is the verse's central word: *ka-annihum lam yaʿsarū dhālika* — "as though they had never dwelt there." The *image* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Cry* is *not a* *Cry that leaves the Thamūd as a memory*; it is *the Cry that erases the evidence of their dwelling*, in the *sense of being* *the sound that leaves the abode empty of the mark of the inhabitants*, in the *sense of being* *the sound that makes the dwelling as if it had never been dwelt in*. The sūrah is stating, in the image, that the *Thamūd* are *not* *destroyed in a way that leaves a trace*; they are *erased* in the *sense of being* *the ones whose dwelling is found as if it had never been*, in the *sense of being* *the ones whose existence is undone in the created order*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *image*, that the *command* is *not a* *destruction of the bodies only*; it is *an erasure of the evidence*, in the way described in *46:25* — the *way* in which the *ʿĀd* are *described* as *such that naught was seen but their dwellings* after the *wind*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *image*, that the *Makkans* who *refuse the sign* are *in the same state*, in the *sense of being* *the ones whose dwelling will be found as if it had never been*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*.

**"Truly Thamūd Disbelieved in Their Lord" — The Reason, Repeated in Its Invariant Form**

The *second* *clause* states the *reason* for the *erasure* in the *same form* stated for the *ʿĀd* in *verse 60*: *inna Thamūda kafarū rabba-hum* — "truly Thamūd disbelieved in their Lord." The *identity of form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *reason* is *not a* *different reason* for *different peoples*; it is *the same reason* in the *sense of being* *the disbelief in the Lord*, in the *sense of being* *the one refusal that the other refusals are forms of*. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *Thamūd's* *hamstringing of the camel* is *not a* *separate sin* from the *ʿĀd's* *following of the tyrant*; it is *the same sin in a different form*, in the way described in *verse 59's statement of the three marks of the ʿĀd's case*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans'* *refusal* is *the same one refusal*, and that the *same one refusal* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *case* is *not over*; it is *in operation* in the *Makkans*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen*.

**"Away with Thamūd!" — The Third Strike of the Refrain**

The *verse* *closes* with the *third* *strike* of the *refrain*: *wailan li-Thamūd* — "away with Thamūd!" The *strike* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Thamūd* are *not* *a people in the distance*; they are *a people done away*, in the *sense of being* *the people that the verdict has spoken over*, in the *sense of being* *the people that the refrain has marked*. The sūrah is stating, in the strike, that the *Thamūd* are *named* in the *refrain* *without the qualification* that the *ʿĀd* received in *verse 60* — the *qualification* of *the people of Hūd* — and that the *named without the qualification* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *refrain* is *not a* *refrain that requires the qualification*; it is *the verdict that stands on the name alone*, in the way described in *verse 44's statement of the refrain struck over the wrongdoing people of Noah*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *strike*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *third* *strike* *applied to the Thamūd*, and that the *applied to the Thamūd* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same case*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next story* — *the story of Abraham* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern* is *not over*; it is *turning to the prophet who is the friend of God*, in the way described in *verse 69*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:69

> **And indeed Our messengers came to Abraham with glad tidings. They said, "Peace." "Peace," he said, and he hastened to bring them a roasted calf.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Our Messengers Came to Abraham with Glad Tidings" — The Story That the Sūrah Turns To**

The sūrah now turns from the stories of the punished peoples to the story of Abraham, and it turns with a form that is different from the forms it has used: *wa-laqad jāʾat rusulunā Ibrāhīma bi-bushrā* — "and indeed our messengers came to Abraham with glad tidings." The *bushrā* — the *glad tidings* — is the *word* that names the *purpose* of the *coming*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *coming* is *not a* *coming of judgment*; it is *a coming of the bushrā*, in the *sense of being* *the act of the messengers who bring the good news*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the sūrah will state in verses 71–73 as the announcement of the birth of Isaac and Jacob*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *story of Abraham* is *not a story of the punished*; it is *the story of the friend of God*, in the way described in *43:67* — the *way* in which the *Qur'an* names *Abraham* as *the friend of God* (*Khalīlat Allāh*). The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *turn*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *story of the one who is the father of the prophets*, and that the *hearing the story* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *idols* are *not* *in the line of Abraham*, in the way described in *verse 26's statement of the call as the whole of the message*.

**"They Said, 'Peace'" — and the Reply: "Peace"**

The *sūrah* *states* the *greeting* of the *messengers* and the *reply* of *Abraham*: *qālū salām* — "they said, peace" — *qāla salām* — "'peace,' he said." The *salām* — the *peace* — is the *word* that the *angels* *use* for the *greeting*, and the *reply* is the *word* that *Abraham* *uses* for the *return of the greeting*, and the *two together* form the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *meeting* is *not a* *meeting of strangers*; it is *a meeting of peace* in the *sense of being* *the meeting that begins with the peace and returns the peace*, in the *sense of being* *the meeting that the angels open and Abraham receives*. The sūrah is stating, in the recording, that the *Abraham* is *not* *afraid* of the *messengers*; he is *the one who returns the peace*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 70's statement of the fear that Abraham will feel when he sees that their hands do not reach the food*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *peace* is *not a* *formality*; it is *the form of the divine meeting*, in the way described in *verse 48's statement of the disembarkation with peace*.

**"And He Hastened to Bring Them a Roasted Calf" — The Hospitality That the Sūrah Records**

The *verse* *closes* with the *act* of the *hospitality*: *fa-saraʿa ilayhim bi-ʿišyān maḥmār* — "and he hastened to bring them a roasted calf." The *saraʿ* — the *hasting*, the *running* — is the *word* that names the *quality* of *Abraham's* *service*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *hospitality* is *not a* *duty performed slowly*; it is *a service performed with haste*, in the *sense of being* *the act of the one who does not delay the guest*, in the *sense of being* *the act of the one who runs to the guest*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Abraham* is *not* *a host in the formal sense*; he is *the host in the full sense*, in the way described in *51:26* — the *way* in which the *calf* is *described* as *a fattened calf* in the *other telling of the story*. The *ʿišyān maḥmār* — the *roasted calf* — is the *word* that names the *specific act of the hospitality*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Abraham* is *not* *serving the guests with a general food*; he is *serving them with the calf that he has prepared*, in the *sense of being* *the host who prepares the specific dish for the specific guests*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *next verses* — the *fear* and the *glad tidings of the wife* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *hospitality* is *not the end of the story*; it is *the ground* on which the *next words* are *spoken*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:70
