The *verse* *closes* with the *statement* that the *sūrah* *gives* with *particular* *weight*: *wa-mā ana ʿalaykum bi-ḥāfiẓ* — "and I am not a keeper over you." The *ḥāfiẓ* — the *keeper*, the *guardian* — is the *word* that the *Qur'an* *uses* for the *guardianship* that the *prophet does not hold* over his *people*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *a guardian of the people's deeds*; he is *the warner who warns*, in the sense of being *the one whose office is the delivery and not the keeping*, in the sense of being *the one who states that the keeping is not his. The *commentators* *note* that the *Qur'an insists* that the *Prophet Muhammad* is *not a keeper or guardian over his people*, citing *4:80; 6:66, 104, 107; 10:108; 17:54; 39:41; 42:6, 48; 88:22*, and that the *recurrence of the statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *office* is *not an office of keeping*; it is *the office of warning*, in the way described in *verse 12's statement of the prophet as only a warner. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *statement of the non-keeping* *applied to their prophet*, and that the *hearing the statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *fate* is *not held by the Prophet*; it is *held by the One who is the keeper of all things*, in the way described in *verse 123's statement of the trust in Him. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *Midianites' mockery* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *promise is not heeded*, in the way described in *verse 87.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:87

> **They said, "O Shuʿayb! Does your manner of praying require that we forsake that which our fathers worshipped, or that we should not do as we will with our wealth? Surely you are a man of forbearance and sound judgment."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Does Your Manner of Praying Require" — The Reframe That the Sūrah Records**

The Midianites' answer to Shuʿayb's call opens with the *reframe* that the sūrah records with particular care: *qālū yā Shuʿaybu aṣ-ṣalātuka taʾmurlanā* — "O Shuʿayb! does your manner of praying require that we…" The *reframe* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *not* *engaging with the command of verse 85*; they are *reclassifying it* in the *sense of being* *the ones who turn the command into a requirement of the prophet's prayer*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who make the prophet's worship the measure of their obligation. The sūrah is stating, in the recording, that the *Midianites* are *not* *refusing the command because it is false*; they are *refusing it because it comes from the prophet's prayer*, in the way described in *verse 62's statement of the Thamūd's appeal to the fathers. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Midianites'* *reframe* is *not a* *new* *reframe*; it is *the reframe of the notables of every story*, in the sense of being *the reframe that places the prophet's authority below the people's customs*, in the sense of being *the reframe that the sūrah has stated in verse 27's statement of the notables' social epistemology.

**"That We Forsake That Which Our Fathers Worshipped" — The Appeal to the Fathers, Repeated**

The *first* *part* of the *reframe* is the *appeal to the fathers* that the *sūrah* *has heard* in *verse 62*: *an naṣraḍa mā ʿabada ābāʾunā* — "that we forsake that which our fathers worshipped." The *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *appeal* is *not a* *Midianite* *invention*; it is *the standing appeal of the idolaters against their prophet*, in the *sense of being* *the appeal that the Thamūd made and the Midianites make*, in the *sense of being* *the appeal that the Makkans make and will make. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Midianites* are *not* *a people who argue from the content of the worship*; they are *the ones who argue from the history of the worship*, in the way described in *2:170's answer to the appeal. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *appeal* *stated to the Shuʿayb*, and that the *hearing the appeal* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *own appeal* is *the same appeal*, in the way described in *verse 109's statement of the worship as the fathers worshipped.

**"Or That We Should Not Do as We Will with Our Wealth" — The Claim of the Wealth**

The *second* *part* of the *reframe* is the *claim of the wealth*: *aw an naqūla fī amwālinā* — "or that we should not do as we will with our wealth." The *claim* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *not* *engaging with the command of the measure*; they are *claiming the wealth as their own property*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who state that the wealth is theirs to do with as they will*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who place the ownership of the wealth above the command of the measure. The sūrah is stating, in the recording, that the *Midianites* are *not* *a people who hold the wealth in trust*; they are *the ones who hold the wealth as their own*, in the way described in *verse 61's statement of the settlement in the earth as a trust. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* who *defraud in their business relations* are *in the same claim*, and that the *in the same claim* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *wealth* is *not their own* in the *sense of being* *the wealth that the Lord holds and allocates*, in the way described in *verse 6's statement of the provision held by God.

**"Surely You Are a Man of Forbearance and Sound Judgment" — The Mockery That the Sūrah Records**

The *verse* *closes* with the *word* that the *commentators* *identify as mockery*: *inna-ka la-min al-sābiqīn* — "surely you are a man of forbearance and sound judgment." The *word* is *not a* *praise* in the *sense of being* *the acknowledgment of the prophet's qualities*; it is *the mockery that states the qualities as the prophet's own opinion of himself*, in the *sense of being* *the word that the Midianites use to dismiss the prophet*, in the *sense of being* *the word that makes the prophet's forbearance and sound judgment the objects of the ridicule. The *commentators* *note* that the *Midianites*, who were *arrogant* (7:88), *did not take seriously Shuʿayb's message*, instead *referring to him as but one of the bewitched* (26:185) *and among the liars* (26:186), and that the *mockery* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *in* the *state of verse 38's mockery of Noah, in the way described throughout the narrative middle. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *Shuʿayb's answer* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mockery is not the end of the story*, in the way described in *verse 88.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:88

> **He said, "O my people! What think you: if I stand upon a clear proof from my Lord, and He has provided me with goodly provision from Himself? I desire not to do behind your backs that which I forbid you. I desire naught but to set matters aright so far as I am able; but my success lies with God alone. In Him do I trust and unto Him do I turn."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"If I Stand Upon a Clear Proof from My Lord" — The Question, Repeated in Its Fourth Form**

Shuʿayb's answer to the Midianites' mockery is the *same* *rhetorical question* that *Noah* asked in *verse 28*, that *Ṣāliḥ* asked in *verse 63*, and that the *sūrah* will *hear* *repeated* in *verse 88*: *yā qawmī a-ʿalaykum in kuntu ʿalā-burhānin min rabbī* — "O my people! what think you: if I stand upon a clear proof from my Lord." The *fourth recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *question* is *not a* *form that the prophets vary*; it is *the form of the prophetic presentation itself*, in the *sense of being* *the form that every prophet takes when he presents the burhān to his mocking people*, in the *sense of being* *the form that the sūrah will state in verse 17 as the standing of the Prophet on the clear proof. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Midianites* are *hearing the same question* that *Noah's people* and the *ʿĀd* and the *Thamūd* *heard*, and that the *hearing the same question* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern is invariant*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *burhān* that the *Shuʿayb* stands upon is *said to be in the form of miracles* (muʿjizāt) *given to all prophets by God to prove their veracity*, and that the *miracles* are the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *burhān is not a theory*; it is *the evidence that the prophet carries*, in the way described in *verse 28's statement of the clear proof.

**"He Has Provided Me with Goodly Provision from Himself" — The Warrant, Stated in a New Form**

The *second clause* of the *answer* states the *warrant* in a *form* that the *previous questions* did not use: *wa-razzakanī minhu rizqā* — "and He has provided me with goodly provision from Himself." The *rizq* — the *provision* — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *used* in *verse 6* for the *provision of every crawling creature*, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Shuʿayb's* *provision* is *not a* *provision that the Midianites give*; it is *the provision that God gives from Himself*, in the *sense of being* *the provision that the prophet holds by the divine grant*, in the *sense of being* *the provision that the Midianites' claim of the wealth in verse 87 does not reach. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *answering the Midianites' claim of the wealth* by a *counter-claim*; he is *stating the source of his own provision*, in the way described in *verse 51's statement of the reward that lies only with the One who originated the prophet. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Midianites'* *claim that the prophet's word is the requirement of his prayer* is *not met by a defense of the prayer*; it is *met by the statement of the provision that is not theirs to give or take*, in the way described in *verse 29's statement of the replacement of the transaction.

**"I Desire Not to Do Behind Your Backs That Which I Forbid You" — The Consistency That the Sūrah States**

The *third clause* states the *consistency* of the *Shuʿayb*: *wa-mā urīdu-khilāfan limā taʿkhalūna* — "I desire not to do behind your backs that which I forbid you." The *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *a prophet who commands what he does not do*; he is *the one who states that his own act is the act that he commands*, in the *sense of being* *the one who does not do in secret what he forbids in public*, in the *sense of being* *the one whose consistency is the ground of his command. The sūrah is stating, in the statement, that the *Midianites'* *accusation* — the *accusation that the prophet's command is a requirement of his prayer rather than a truth for the people* — is *not met by a better argument*; it is *met by the statement of the consistency*, in the way described in *verse 31's statement of the prophet's denials. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *statement of the consistency* *applied to their prophet*, and that the *hearing the statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet's* *act is the act that he commands*, in the way described in *the ḥadīth of Muslim on the actions according to the intentions.

**"My Success Lies with God Alone" — and the Trust That Closes the Answer**

The *final* *clauses* state the *tawakkul* that *closes* the *answer*: *wa-mā tawfīqī illā bi-llāhi ʿalayhi tawakkaltu wa-ilayhi unīb* — "but my success lies with God alone. In Him do I trust and unto Him do I turn." The *tawfīq* — the *success*, the *guidance to the right* — is the *word* that the *commentators* *note* is *a well-known expression in everyday Muslim discourse*, employed *in many diverse contexts*, and that the *expression* conveys *the idea that no matter what one does, it is ultimately up to God whether it will be successful and carry a blessing in this world and in the next*. The sūrah is stating, in the expression, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *claiming the success for himself*; he is *placing it with God*, in the way described in *verse 56's statement of the trust in the Lord. The *second* *clause* — *the trust and the turning* — is *identical to the statement that the Prophet is taught to make in 42:10*, and the *identity* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *tawakkul* is *not a* *Shuʿayb's individual act*; it is *the form of the prophetic tawakkul itself*, in the way described in *verse 123's statement of the trust in Him. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — the *warning of the previous peoples* and the *Midianites' answer* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *tawakkul is not the end of the story*, in the way described in *verses 89–90.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:89

> **O my people! Let not your dissension with me lead you into sin, such that there should befall you that which befell the people of Noah, or the people of Hūd, or the people of Ṣāliḥ; and the people of Lot are not far from you!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Let Not Your Dissension with Me Lead You into Sin" — The Warning That the Sūrah Gives**

Shuʿayb's next word to his people is the *warning* that the sūrah gives with particular weight: *yā qawmī lā yaʾtikum khaṣmukum an taʿmalū* — "O my people! let not your dissension with me lead you into sin." The *warning* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites'* *dissension* — the *mockery of verse 87*, the *reframe of the command*, the *claim of the wealth* — is *not a* *harmless* *act*; it is *the act that leads into the sin*, in the *sense of being* *the act that produces the fraud and the corruption of verse 85*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the warning addresses in its causal form. The sūrah is stating, in the warning, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *asking his people to stop the dissension out of respect for him*; he is *asking them to stop it because it leads into the sin*, in the way described in *5:2 and 5:8* — the *way* in which the *Qur'an* commands *not letting anger or resentment, even when justified, lead one to sin*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans'* *dissension with the Prophet* is *the same dissension*, and that the *same dissension* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *mockery* is *not a* *harmless* *act*; it is *the act that leads into the sin of the fraud and the harm to the believers*, in the way described in *verse 38's statement of the mockery that the believers will laugh at.

**"That There Should Befall You That Which Befell the People of Noah" — and the Naming of the Four Peoples**

The *second* *part* of the *warning* is the *naming* of the *four peoples* that the *dissension will bring upon the Midianites if it continues*: *fa-yumsakākum mā amsaka qawma Nūḥin wa-qawma Hūdin wa-qawma Ṣāliḥ* — "such that there should befall you that which befell the people of Noah, or the people of Hūd, or the people of Ṣāli." The *naming of the three peoples* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *warning his people by a general threat*; he is *warning them by the specific names of the specific peoples that the sūrah has just narrated*, in the *sense of being* *the one who says to them: the flood that took the people of Noah, the wind that took the people of Hūd, the cry that took the people of Ṣāliḥ — these are the forms that your dissension will bring upon you*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *inventing the threat*; he is *citing the history that the sūrah has just stated*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *naming of the four peoples* *applied to their own dissension*, and that the *applied to their own dissension* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *fate* is *not a* *new* *fate*; it is *the fate of the four peoples*, in the way described in *verse 109's statement of the share undiminished.

**"And the People of Lot Are Not Far from You" — The Word That the Sūrah Adds**

The *final* *clause* of the *warning* is the *word* that the *sūrah* adds with *particular* *weight*: *wa-qawmu Lūṭin lā bayʿadakum* — "and the people of Lot are not far from you!" The *word* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *people of Lot* are *not* *a people in the distance* from the *Midianites*; they are *the most recent and the closest in time* of the *destroyed peoples* to the *Midianites*, in the *sense of being* *the people whose destruction is the nearest in the history that the Midianites can observe*, in the *sense of being* *the people whose stones are the nearest in the geography that the Midianites can see. The sūrah is stating, in the word, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *warning his people by a distant history*; he is *warning them by the nearest history*, in the way described in *verse 83's statement of the stones that are never far from the wrongdoers. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *word of the nearness* *applied to their own history*, and that the *applied to their own history* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *nearest destroyed people* are *the peoples that the sūrah has just narrated*, in the way described in *verse 100's statement of the stories of the towns. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *call to seek forgiveness* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *warning is not the end of the word*, in the way described in *verse 90.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:90

> **And seek forgiveness from your Lord; then turn unto Him in repentance. Truly my Lord is Merciful, Loving."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"And Seek Forgiveness from Your Lord; Then Turn unto Him in Repentance" — The Form, Repeated in Its Fourth Occurrence**

The sūrah now states the *practical form of the call* in the *Shuʿayb's* *mouth*: *wa-staghfirū rabbakum thumma ūbū ilayhi* — "and seek forgiveness from your Lord; then turn unto him in repentance." This is the *fourth occurrence* of the *form* in the *sūrah*: the *first* in *verse 3*, the *second* in *verse 52*, the *third* in *verse 61*, and the *fourth* here, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *form* is *not a* *form that the prophets vary*; it is *the constant of the call itself*, in the *sense of being* *the form that every prophet gives when he calls his people to the conversion*, in the *sense of being* *the form that the Makkans are being asked to perform now and that the Midianites were asked to perform then. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *ending his warning with the threat*; he is *ending it with the practical form of the turning*, in the way described in *verse 3's statement of the connection between forgiveness and repentance. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *form* *stated to the Midianites*, and that the *hearing the form stated to the Midianites* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same case*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen.

**"Truly My Lord is Merciful, Loving" — The Closing That Names the Two Attributes**

The *verse* *closes* with the *naming* of the *two* *attributes* that *govern* the *form*: *innī rabbi raʾūf raḥīm* — "truly my Lord is Merciful, Loving." The *raḥīm* — the *Merciful* — and the *raʾūf* — the *Loving*, the *tender*, the *one who loves with the love of the one who is near* — are the *two attributes* that the *Shuʿayb* *names* for the *Lord* in this *closing*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *form of the turning* is *not a* *form that the Lord receives without the mercy and the love*, in the *sense of being* *the form that the mercy governs and the love holds*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *ending his warning with the threat of verse 89*; he is *ending it with the mercy and the love of the Lord*, in the way described in *verse 73's statement of the mercy and the blessings be upon you. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *naming of the mercy and the love* *applied to their Lord*, and that the *hearing the naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *turning* is *not a* *turning to a Lord who is only the Strong and the Mighty of verse 66*; it is *the turning to the Lord who is the Merciful and the Loving*, in the way described in *verse 41's statement of the forgiving and the merciful. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *Midianites' final answer* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mercy and the love are not heeded*, in the way described in *verse 91.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:91

> **They said, "O Shuʿayb! We do not comprehend much of what you say, and verily we consider you to be weak among us. And were it not for your clan, we would surely stone you, for you have no power over us."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"We Do Not Comprehend Much of What You Say" — The Derision That the Sūrah Records**

The Midianites' final answer to Shuʿayb's call opens with the *derision* that the sūrah records with particular care: *qālū yā Shuʿaybu mā nafqah mimmā taqūlu* — "O Shuʿayb! we do not comprehend much of what you say." The *derision* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *not* *refusing the message because they have considered it and found it false*; they are *refusing it by the statement of their own incomprehension*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who state that they do not understand the prophet's words*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who make the incomprehension the form of the refusal. The sūrah is stating, in the recording, that the *Midianites* are *not* *a people who engage with the content*; they are *the ones who state their own incomprehension as the reason*, in the way described in *verse 20's statement of the spiritual deafness and blindness. The *commentators* *note* that the *statement of the incomprehension* is *a way of expressing derision for him*, and that the *derision* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *in* the *state of verse 38's mockery of Noah, in the way described throughout the narrative middle.

**"And Verily We Consider You to Be Weak Among Us" — and the Word That the Sūrah Records**

The *second* *clause* of the *answer* is the *statement* of the *Midianites'* *valuation* of the *Shuʿayb*: *wa-la-naʿtadūka fīnā ʿaẓafā* — "and verily we consider you to be weak among us." The *ʿaẓf* — the *weakness*, the *lowness* — is the *word* that names the *Midianites'* *valuation* of the *Shuʿayb*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *not* *refusing the message because of its content*; they are *refusing it because of the prophet's status in their estimation*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who value the prophet by the social measure*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who state that the prophet is weak among them. The sūrah is stating, in the recording, that the *Midianites* are *in* the *state of verse 27's statement of the notables' social epistemology, in the way described throughout the narrative middle. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* who *despised the believers of low status* are *in the same valuation*, and that the *in the same valuation* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *valuation* is *not the measure that counts*, in the way described in *verse 31's statement of the valuation of God.

**"And Were It Not for Your Clan, We Would Surely Stone You" — The Threat That the Sūrah Records**

The *third* *clause* is the *threat*: *wa-lawlā ʿashīratuka larajamanāk* — "and were it not for your clan, we would surely stone you." The *threat* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *not* *refusing the message by a general statement*; they are *refusing it by the specific threat of the stoning*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who state the act that they would do to the prophet if they could*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who hold the prophet back by the clan. The *commentators* *note* that in *ancient Arabian tribal culture* people *were offered protection by their clan*, and that *the clan to which Shuʿayb belonged was a part of the religious community of the Midianites*, so that *they could not hurt him*, and that the *protection of the clan* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *safe by his own power*; he is *safe by the clan*, in the way described in *verse 80's statement of the Lot's wish for the mighty support. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *threat of the stoning* *applied to their prophet*, and that the *hearing the threat* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *threats against the Prophet and the believers* are *the same threats*, in the way described in *verse 38's statement of the mockery.

**"For You Have No Power over Us" — The Closing That the Sūrah Gives**

The *verse* *closes* with the *statement* that the *sūrah* gives with *particular* *weight*: *wa-mā lakunā ʿalaynā min quwwah* — "for you have no power over us." The *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *not* *refusing the message by the threat only*; they are *refusing it by the statement of their own power*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who state that the prophet has no power over them*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who place their own power above the prophetic authority. The sūrah is stating, in the statement, that the *Midianites* are *in* the *state of verse 55's statement of the ʿĀd's scheme against Hūd, in the way described throughout the narrative middle. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *Shuʿayb's final answer* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *statement of the power is not the end of the story*, in the way described in *verse 92.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:92

> **He said, "O my people! Is my clan mightier over you than God? Yet, you would put Him behind you with disregard. Surely my Lord encompasses whatsoever you do."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Is My Clan Mightier over You than God?" — The Question That the Sūrah Records**

Shuʿayb's answer to the Midianites' statement of the clan is the *question* that the sūrah records with particular weight: *qāla yā qawmī a-ʿashīratī aʿaẓumu ʿalaykum min Allāh* — "O my people! is my clan mightier over you than God?" The *question* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *defending the clan*; he is *placing the clan and God in the same question*, in the *sense of being* *the one who asks the Midianites to compare the two*, in the *sense of being* *the one who states that the clan is not the measure of the might. The sūrah is stating, in the question, that the *Midianites'* *protection of the Shuʿayb by the clan* is *not a* *protection that the prophet claims*; it is *the protection that the Midianites hold*, and the *question* is *the turning of the protection against the holders*, in the way described in *verse 30's statement of the question that inverts the frame. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* who *hold the Prophet back by the clan* are *hearing the question addressed to their own holding*, and that the *hearing the question addressed to the holding* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *clan is not the measure of the might*, in the way described in *verse 56's statement of the trust in the Lord.

**"Yet, You Would Put Him Behind You with Disregard" — and the Word That the Sūrah Names**

The *second* *clause* is the *word* that the *sūrah* names with *particular* *care*: *wa-tatakhayhillūhu anduḥā* — "yet, you would put Him behind you with disregard." The *word* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *not* *forgetting God in the sense of not knowing Him*; they are *putting Him behind them* in the *sense of being* *the ones who cast God behind their backs and are not fearful of Him*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who place God behind the clan and the wealth and the power. The *commentators* *note* that the *word* is *understood to mean* "You would cast God behind your backs and not be fearful of Him?", and that the *casting behind the backs* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites'* *refusal* is *not a* *refusal of the unknown*; it is *the refusal of the known who is placed behind*, in the way described in *verse 5's statement of the folded breast. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *naming*, that the *Makkans* who *put God behind the clan and the wealth* are *in the same casting*, and that the *in the same casting* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *refusal* is *not a* *refusal of the unknown*; it is *the refusal of the known who is placed behind*, in the way described in *verse 109's statement of the worship as the fathers worshipped.

**"Surely My Lord Encompasses Whatsoever You Do" — The Closing That the Sūrah Gives**

The *verse* *closes* with the *statement* that the *sūrah* gives with *particular* *weight*: *wa-inna rabbi latāwī mā taʿmalūn* — "surely my Lord encompasses whatsoever you do." The *tawī* — the *encompassing* — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *used* in *verse 57* for the *Lord's watching over all things*, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites'* *acts* — the *mockery*, the *threat*, the *statement of the power* — are *not* *outside* the *encompassing*, in the *sense of being* *the acts that the Lord holds in the encompassing*, in the *sense of being* *the acts that the account will reach. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *ending his answer with the question*; he is *ending it with the statement of the encompassing*, in the way described in *verse 5's statement of the knowledge of what lies within breasts. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *statement of the encompassing* *applied to their own acts*, and that the *hearing the statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *mockery and threats* are *not* *outside* the *encompassing*, in the way described in *verse 123's statement of the Lord who is not heedless of that which you do. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *word of the mutual watching* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *encompassing is not the end of the story*, in the way described in *verse 93.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:93

> **O my people! Act according to your position; I, too, am acting. Soon you shall know upon whom there comes a punishment that disgraces him, and who is a liar. So keep watch! I, too, am among you watching."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Act According to Your Position; I, Too, Am Acting" — The Word That the Sūrah Will Hear Again**

The sūrah now states Shuʿayb's *final word* to his people, and it states it in the *form* that the sūrah *will state again* in *verses 121–122* as the *Prophet's own word to the Makkans*: *yā qawmī aʿmalū inī ʿāmil* — "O my people! act according to your position; I, too, am acting." The *form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *arguing with the Midianites any further*; he is *stating the mutual position* in the *sense of being* *the one who says to them: do what you will do, and I will do what I will do*, in the *sense of being* *the one who places the two positions side by side without the further argument. The *commentators* *note* that the *word* is *similar to what the Prophet is instructed to tell the Makkan idolaters* (6:135; 39:39), and that the *similarity* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Shuʿayb's word* is *the Prophet's word*, in the *sense of being* *the form of the prophetic word at the end of the encounter*, in the *sense of being* *the word that the sūrah will give to the Prophet in verses 121–122. The sūrah is stating, in the form, that the *Makkans* are *hearing their own word spoken by the Shuʿayb to the Midianites*, and that the *hearing their own word* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same case*, in the way described throughout the *narrative middle*.

**"Soon You Shall Know Upon Whom There Comes a Punishment That Disgraces Him" — and the Word of the Knowing**

The *second* *part* of the *word* is the *statement of the knowing*: *qarīban taʿlamūna ʿalā may yāʾī ʿadhābu-l-yawm alladhī yunkhisī* — "soon you shall know upon whom there comes a punishment that disgraces him." The *qarīb* — the *soon* — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *has used* in *verse 39* and *verse 64* for the *proximity of the account*, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *not a* *distant* *event*; it is *near* in the *sense of being* *within the time of the Midianites and the Shuʿayb alike*, in the *sense of being* *the event that the knowledge will reach. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *stating a prediction*; he is *stating the proximity*, in the way described in *verse 8's statement of the muddat. The *word* — *the knowing* — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Midianites* are *not* *safe by the distance of the account*; they are *within the range of the knowing*, in the way described in *verse 39's statement of the punishment that disgraces.

**"And Who Is a Liar" — The Word That the Sūrah Adds**

The *next* *clause* adds the *word* that the *sūrah* gives with *particular* *weight*: *wa-may kanna dhālika kathībā* — "and who is a liar." The *clause* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *knowing* is *not a* *knowing of the punishment only*; it is *the knowing of the liar* in the *sense of being* *the knowing that will distinguish the true from the false*, in the *sense of being* *the knowing that the Midianites will not avoid. The sūrah is stating, in the clause, that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *ending his word with the threat*; he is *ending it with the statement of the knowing that will reach the truth and the falsehood together*, in the way described in *verse 35's statement of the accusation of fabrication. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *word of the knowing of the liar* *applied to their own accusation*, and that the *hearing the word* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *accusation* is *not a* *accusation that will survive the knowing*, in the way described in *verse 13's statement of the challenge.

**"So Keep Watch! I, Too, Am Among You Watching" — The Closing That the Sūrah Gives**

The *verse* *closes* with the *word* that the *sūrah* gives with *particular* *weight*: *fatraḍḍabū innī mardḍab* — "so keep watch! I, too, am among you watching." The *muraḍḍabah* — the *mutual watching* — is the *word* that the *commentators* *note* is *the form of the prophetic word at the end of the encounter*, and that the *form* is *the sūrah's way of stating* that the *Shuʿayb* is *not* *leaving the Midianites without a word*; he is *stating that he is watching them as they are watching him*, in the *sense of being* *the one who places the two watchings side by side*, in the *sense of being* *the one who states that the watching is mutual. The *commentators* *note* that the *Prophet is told to speak similar words to those who do not believe in his message* (9:52; 10:20, 102; 32:30), and that the *similarity* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Shuʿayb's closing* is *the Prophet's closing*, in the way described in *verses 121–122. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *word of the mutual watching* *applied to their own watching of the Prophet*, and that the *hearing the word* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *not watching the Prophet only*; they are *being watched*, in the way described in *verse 123's statement of the Lord who is not heedless. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — the *coming of the command and the Cry* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mutual watching is not the end of the story*, in the way described in *verses 94–95.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:94

> **And when Our Command came, We saved Shuʿayb and those who believed with him, through a Mercy from Us. And the Cry seized those who did wrong, and morning found them lying lifeless in their abode,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"And When Our Command Came" — The Command, Stated in Its Fifth Form**

The sūrah states the *coming of the command* for *Midian* in the *same form* that it *stated* for *Noah* in *verse 40*, for the *ʿĀd* in *verse 58*, for the *Thamūd* in *verse 66*, and for the *people of Lot* in *verse 82*: *fa-lammā jāʾa amruna* — "and when Our command came." The *fifth recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* is *not a* *different act* for *different peoples*; it is *the same act* in the *sense of being* *the act that comes at the appointed time*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the tryst of verse 81 holds and executes for the people of Lot and the command of verse 89 holds and executes for Midian. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *command* is *not a* *mechanical* *event*; it is *the act of the divine word upon the created order*, in the way described in *verse 1's statement of the determined and expounded Book. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *statement of the command* *applied to Midian*, and that the *applied to Midian* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same command*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen.

**"We Saved Shuʿayb and Those Who Believed with Him, through a Mercy from Us" — The Salvation, Repeated in Its Invariant Form**

The *second* *clause* states the *salvation* in the *same form* stated for *Noah* in *verse 40*, for *Hūd* in *verse 58*, and for *Ṣāli* in *verse 66*: *najjainā Shuʿayban wa-man āmana maʿahu bi-raḥmatin minnā* — "we saved Shuʿayb and those who believed with him through a mercy from us." The *identity of form across the four salvations* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *salvation* is *not a* *different kind of act* for *different prophets*; it is *the same act* in the *sense of being* *the act of the mercy that carries the believers through the command*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the command does not reach. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *believers of Midian* are *not* *a different kind of few* from the *believers of the other peoples*; they are *the same few* in the *way* *described* in *verse 40's statement of the few who believed. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans'* *believers* are *the same few*, and that the *same few* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *salvation* is *not a* *past event*; it is *the form of the salvation that the Makkans are being offered now*, in the way described in *verse 23's statement of the inhabitants of the Garden.

**"And the Cry Seized Those Who Did Wrong" — and the Form of the Command for Midian**

The *third* *clause* states the *form of the command* for *Midian*: *wa-akhadha-l-ladhīna ẓalamū aṣ-ṣayḥah* — "and the Cry seized those who did wrong." The *ṣayah* — the *Cry* — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *used* in *verse 67* for the *form of the command* for the *Thamūd*, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* for *Midian* is *the same form* as the *command* for the *Thamūd*, in the *sense of being* *the terrible loud sound that seizes the wrongdoers*, in the *sense of being* *the sound that the morning finds completed. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Shuʿayb's warning of verse 89* — the *warning by the specific names of the specific peoples* — is *not a* *warning that the Midianites can avoid*; it is *the warning that the form of the command holds*, in the way described in *verse 89's statement of the naming of the four peoples. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *statement of the Cry* *applied to Midian*, and that the *applied to Midian* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same Cry*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen.

**"And Morning Found Them Lying Lifeless in Their Abode" — The Image, Repeated in Its Invariant Form**

The *final* *clause* states the *image* of the *state of the seized* in the *same form* stated for the *Thamūd* in *verse 67*: *wa-ṣabaū fī dāri-him jāmidīn* — "and morning found them lying lifeless in their abode." The *identity of the image* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* for *Midian* is *not a* *different kind of destruction* from the *command* for the *Thamūd*; it is *the same destruction* in the *sense of being* *the destruction that the morning finds in the state of the lifeless*, in the *sense of being* *the destruction that leaves the abode as the abode of the lifeless. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *image* *applied to Midian*, and that the *applied to Midian* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *abode* is *in the same image*, in the way described in *verse 100's statement of the stories of the towns. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *as though they had never dwelt there* and the *refrain* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *image is not the end of the description*, in the way described in *verse 95.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:95

> **as though they had never dwelt there. Behold! Away with Midian, just as Thamūd was done away!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"As Though They Had Never Dwelt There" — The Image, Repeated in Its Invariant Form**

The sūrah completes the description of Midian with the *image* that it *used* for the *Thamūd* in *verse 68*: *ka-ʾannihum lam yaʿsarū dhālika* — "as though they had never dwelt there." The *identity of the image* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *destruction of Midian* is *not a* *different kind of erasure* from the *destruction of the Thamūd*; it is *the same erasure* in the *sense of being* *the erasure that leaves the abode as if it had never been dwelt in*, in the *sense of being* *the erasure that the morning finds completed. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *image* *applied to Midian*, and that the *applied to Midian* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *abode* is *in the same erasure*, in the way described in *verse 100's statement of the stories of the towns. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *not* *hearing a foreign image*; they are *hearing the image of their own possible abode*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen.

**"Away with Midian" — The Fourth Strike of the Refrain**

The *sūrah* now *strikes* the *refrain* for the *fourth* *time*: *wailan li-Madyana ka-mā bāt Thamūd* — "away with Midian, just as Thamūd was done away!" The *strike* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that *Midian* is *not a* *people in the distance*; it is *a people done away*, in the *sense of being* *the people that the verdict has spoken over*, in the *sense of being* *the people that the refrain has marked. The sūrah is stating, in the strike, that the *Midian* are *named in the refrain with the reference to the Thamūd*, and that the *named with the reference* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *fates of the two peoples are so similar* that the *refrain names one by the other*, in the way described in *7:91–92. The *four strikes together* — *the people of Noah* in *verse 44*, the *ʿĀd* in *verse 60*, the *Thamūd* in *verse 68*, and *Midian* in *this verse* — form the *sūrah's* *complete verdict on the pattern of refusal*, and the *complete verdict* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern* is *not a* *series of separate events*; it is *one pattern* in the *sense of being* *the pattern that the four strikes mark.

**"Just as Thamūd Was Done Away" — and the Word That the Sūrah Gives**

The *final* *clause* is the *word* that the *sūrah* gives with *particular* *weight*: *ka-mā bāt Thamūd* — "just as Thamūd was done away." The *word* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *fate of Midian* is *not a* *new fate*; it is *the fate of the Thamūd* in the *sense of being* *the fate that the refrain names by the reference*, in the *sense of being* *the fate that the sūrah has just described in verse 68. The sūrah is stating, in the word, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *word of the done away* *applied to Midian by the Thamūd*, and that the *applied by the Thamūd* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *fate* is *not a* *new fate*; it is *the fate of the four peoples*, in the way described in *verse 109's statement of the share undiminished. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — *the story of Moses* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern is not over* by the *fourth strike*; it is *turning to the prophet of Pharaoh*, in the way described in *verses 96–97.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:96

> **And indeed We sent Moses with Our signs and a manifest authority**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"And Indeed We Sent Moses" — The Story That the Sūrah Turns To**

The sūrah now opens the story of Moses with the same form it has used throughout the narrative middle: *wa-laqad arsalnā Mūsā bi-āyātinā wa-sulṭānin mubīn* — "and indeed We sent Moses with Our signs and a manifest authority." The *commentators* *note* that the *story of Moses* is *one of the most extensive stories in the Qur'an*, appearing in *2:49–61; 7:103–55; 10:75–93; 20:9–97; 26:10–66; 28:3–46; 79:15–25*, and that the *appearance here* is *the sūrah's way of stating* that the *Moses* is *the last of the seven prophets of the narrative middle*, in the *sense of being* *the prophet who closes the gallery*. The sūrah is stating, in the opening, that the *Moses* is *not a* *prophet of a different kind* from the *prophets of the narrative middle*; he is *the one sent with the signs and the authority*, in the way described in *verse 25's statement of the sending of Noah. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *story of the Moses* *in the same form* as the *stories of the other prophets*, and that the *in the same form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *prophet* is *in the same form*, in the way described in *verse 2's statement of the warner on God's behalf.

**"With Our Signs" — and the Word That the Sūrah Names**

The *second* *clause* states the *signs* with which the *Moses* was *sent*: *bi-āyātinā* — "with Our signs." The *āyāt* — the *signs* — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *used* in *verse 59* for the *signs that the ʿĀd rejected*, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *signs of the Moses* are *the same kind of signs* as the *signs of the other prophets*, in the *sense of being* *the miracles that the prophets perform by the divine grant*, in the *sense of being* *the evidence that the command carries. The *commentators* *note* that the *signs* are *the miracles he performed*, and that the *miracles* are *the sūrah's way of stating* that the *Moses* is *not a* *prophet who claims the authority by his own power*; he is *the one who carries the signs of the Lord*, in the way described in *verse 88's statement of the clear proof in the form of the miracles. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *naming*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *statement of the signs* *applied to the Moses*, and that the *applied to the Moses* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *demand for a sign* — the *demand of verse 12* — is *not a* *demand that the sūrah can meet by the signs of the Moses*; it is *the demand that the sūrah answers by the statement of the signs that the Moses carried*, in the way described in *verse 13's statement of the challenge.

**"And a Manifest Authority" — The Word That the Sūrah Gives**

The *final* *clause* states the *authority* that the *Moses* was *sent with*: *wa-sulṭānin mubīn* — "and a manifest authority." The *sulṭān* — the *authority*, the *proof*, the *power* — is the *word* that the *commentators* *note* some *state* refers to *Moses' staff specifically*, and that the *staff* is *the sūrah's way of stating* that the *authority* is *not a* *abstract* *concept*; it is *the specific object that the command carries*, in the *sense of being* *the staff that the Moses carries and that the command effects through*. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Moses* is *not a* *prophet who carries the authority in the abstract*; he is *the one who carries the manifest authority in the specific*, in the way described in *7:106–108* — the *way* in which the *Qur'an* states the *staff of the Moses*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *statement of the manifest authority* *applied to the Moses*, and that the *applied to the Moses* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *prophet* is *sent with the manifest authority* in the *same way*, in the way described in *verse 17's statement of the one who stands upon the clear proof. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — *the notables of Pharaoh* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *authority is not over by the sending*, in the way described in *verse 97.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:97

> **unto Pharaoh and his notables. But they followed the command of Pharaoh, and the command of Pharaoh was not sound.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Unto Pharaoh and His Notables" — The Addressees, Named**

The sūrah now states the *addressees* of the *Moses*: *ilā Firʿawna wa-malaʾi-hi* — "unto Pharaoh and his notables." The *naming of the two* — the *Pharaoh* and the *notables* — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Moses* is *not sent to a single person*; he is *sent to the structure* in the *sense of being* *the one who is addressed to the ruler and his court*, in the *sense of being* *the one who is addressed to the power and the elite together. The *commentators* *note* that the *notables of Pharaoh* are *described in 7:103 and 10:75*, and that the *naming of the notables* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *refusal of the Moses* is *not a* *refusal of the Pharaoh alone*; it is *the refusal of the structure*, in the way described in *verse 59's statement of the following of the stubborn tyrant. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Makkans'* *notables* are *the notables of the Makkans' Pharaoh*, and that the *being the notables* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *refusal* is *not a* *refusal of the individual*; it is *the refusal of the structure*, in the way described in *verse 27's statement of the notables' social epistemology.

**"But They Followed the Command of Pharaoh" — The Following, Stated**

The *second* *clause* states the *following*: *fa-attabaʿū amra Firʿawn* — "but they followed the command of Pharaoh." The *ittibāʿ* — the *following* — is the *word* that names the *relation of the notables to the Pharaoh*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables* are *not* *independent* in their *refusal*; they are *the ones who follow the command of the Pharaoh*, in the *sense of being* *the ones whose refusal is the refusal of the Pharaoh*, in the *sense of being* *the ones who place the command of the Pharaoh above the signs of the Lord. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *notables* are *in* the *state of verse 59's statement of the following of the stubborn tyrant, in the way described throughout the narrative middle. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans'* *notables* are *in the same following*, and that the *in the same following* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *refusal* is *not a* *refusal of the individual notables*; it is *the following of the command of the structure*, in the way described in *verse 59's statement of the three marks of the ʿĀd's case.

**"And the Command of Pharaoh Was Not Sound" — The Characterization That the Sūrah Gives**

The *final* *clause* states the *characterization* of the *command*: *wa-amru Firʿawn lā wasīf* — "and the command of Pharaoh was not sound." The *wasīf* — the *soundness*, the *rightness* — is the *word* that names the *quality* that the *command* lacks, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command of the Pharaoh* is *not a* *command that the notables can follow without the loss of the soundness*, in the *sense of being* *the command that is not sound in its source*, in the *sense of being* *the command that the sūrah characterizes as the not sound. The sūrah is stating, in the characterization, that the *notables* are *not* *following a sound command*; they are *following the command that is not sound*, in the way described in *verse 56's statement of the Lord who is upon the straight path. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans'* *notables* who *follow the command of the structure* are *following the command that is not sound*, and that the *following the not sound* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *refusal* is *not a* *refusal that the notables can defend by the soundness of the command*, in the way described in *verse 59's statement of the following of the stubborn tyrant. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — *the leading of the people to the Fire* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *following of the not sound is not the end of the story*, in the way described in *verses 98–99.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:98

> **He shall go before his people on the Day of Resurrection and lead them to the Fire. Evil indeed is the watering place to which they are led!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"He Shall Go Before His People" — The Leading, Stated for the Day of Resurrection**

The sūrah now states the *fate* of the *Pharaoh and his notables* for the *Day of Resurrection*: *yasbi qawmahu yawma-l-qiyāmah* — "he shall go before his people on the Day of Resurrection." The *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Pharaoh* is *not* *a leader in this world only*; he is *the leader of his people in the Hereafter* in the *sense of being* *the one who goes before them*, in the *sense of being* *the one who leads them to the destination that the command of verse 97 holds. The *commentators* *note* that the *statement* is *in accordance with 17:71* — the *way* in which the *Qur'an* states: "On the Day We shall call every people by their imam," and that the *calling by the imam* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *leadership* is *not a* *leadership that ends at the death*; it is *the leadership that the Day of Resurrection carries forward*. The sūrah is stating, in the statement, that the *Pharaoh's* *leadership* is *not a* *leadership of the sound command of verse 97*; it is *the leadership of the not sound*, and that the *leadership of the not sound* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Pharaoh* is *not* *leading his people to the good*; he is *leading them to the Fire*, in the way described in *verse 59's statement of the following of the stubborn tyrant.

**"And Lead Them to the Fire" — and the Word That the Sūrah Gives**

The *second* *clause* states the *destination* of the *leading*: *wa-yahdīhim ilā-n-nār* — "and lead them to the Fire." The *Fire* is the *word* that the *sūrah* *has used* throughout for the *destination of the wrongdoers* (vv. 16, 17, 22, 94), and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Pharaoh and his notables* are *not* *a people with a different destination* from the *people of the narrative middle*; they are *the ones whose destination is the Fire*, in the *sense of being* *the ones that the leadership of the not sound carries to the Fire*, in the *sense of being* *the ones that the following of verse 97 holds in the Fire. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Makkans'* *notables* who *follow the command of the structure* are *in the same leading*, and that the *in the same leading* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *destination* is *not a* *destination that the notables can choose by their following*, in the way described in *verse 16's statement of the Fire as the remainder of the account. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *statement of the leading to the Fire* *applied to the Pharaoh and his notables*, and that the *applied to the Pharaoh and his notables* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *notables* are *in the same leading*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen.

**"Evil Indeed Is the Watering Place to Which They Are Led" — The Word That the Sūrah Closes With**

The *verse* *closes* with the *word* that the *sūrah* gives with *particular* *weight*: *sawāʾin sabānin yuhdāna ilayh* — "evil indeed is the watering place to which they are led!" The *wasīf* — the *watering place*, the *share*, the *portion* — is the *word* that the *commentators* *note* is *usually associated with the quenching of thirst*, and that the *association* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Fire* is *not a* *destination that the wrongdoers can approach without the thirst*, in the *sense of being* *the place that the wrongdoers are led to in the state of the thirst that the Fire does not quench*, in the *sense of being* *the place that the sūrah characterizes as the evil watering place. The sūrah is stating, in the word, that the *destination* is *not a* *destination that the wrongdoers can approach with the satisfaction*; it is *the destination that the sūrah characterizes as the evil*, in the way described in *verse 39's statement of the punishment that endures. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *curse in this world and the Day of Resurrection* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *evil watering place is not the end of the description*, in the way described in *verse 99.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:99

> **And they were pursued in this [world] by a curse, and [shall be] on the Day of Resurrection. Evil indeed is the gift that will be offered them!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"They Were Pursued in This World by a Curse" — The Double Verdict, Repeated in Its Invariant Form**

The sūrah states the *fate* of the *Pharaoh and his notables* in the *same form* stated for the *ʿĀd* in *verse 60*: *wa-laḥiqat-humu-l-laʿnatu fī hādhihi wa- yawma-l-qiyāmah* — "they were pursued in this world by a curse, and [shall be] on the Day of Resurrection." The *identity of the double verdict* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *fate of the Pharaoh and his notables* is *not a* *different kind of fate* from the *fate of the ʿĀd*; it is *the same fate* in the *sense of being* *the fate that is pursued in this world and in the next*, in the *sense of being* *the fate that the curse holds in the two times. The sūrah is stating, in the identity, that the *Pharaoh and his notables* are *not* *a people with a different curse* from the *ʿĀd*; they are *the ones whom the same curse pursues*, in the way described in *verse 18's statement of the curse as exile from the mercy. The *commentators* *note* that the *curse in this world* can *refer to a curse in general* or *specifically to their being drowned in the Red Sea*, and that the *drowning in the Red Sea* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *curse in this world* is *not a* *abstract* *curse*; it is *the specific event that the curse holds*, in the way described in *7:136. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *double verdict* *applied to the Pharaoh and his notables*, and that the *applied to the Pharaoh and his notables* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same double verdict*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen.

**"Evil Indeed Is the Gift That Will Be Offered Them" — The Word That the Sūrah Closes With**

The *verse* *closes* with the *word* that the *sūrah* gives with *particular* *weight*: *sawāʾin ʿaṭāʾin yuʿṭāna* — "evil indeed is the gift that will be offered them!" The *ʿaṭā* — the *gift*, the *offering* — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *uses* for the *destination* of the *wrongdoers* in the *form of the offering*, and the *use* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *destination* is *not a* *destination that the wrongdoers can receive without the evil*, in the *sense of being* *the offering that the sūrah characterizes as the evil*, in the *sense of being* *the gift that the curse of the double verdict holds. The sūrah is stating, in the word, that the *Makkans'* *destination* — the *destination that the Makkans' refusal holds* — is *not a* *destination that the sūrah characterizes as the good*, in the way described in *verse 23's statement of the inhabitants of the Garden. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *word of the evil offering* *applied to the Pharaoh and his notables*, and that the *applied to the Pharaoh and his notables* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *offering* is *the same offering*, in the way described in *verse 109's statement of the share undiminished. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *statement of the stories of the towns* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *narrative middle is not over* by the *story of Moses*, in the way described in *verses 100–102.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:100

> **These are among the stories We have recounted unto thee of the towns. Among them are some that remain and some that have been mown down.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"These Are Among the Stories We Have Recounted unto Thee of the Towns" — The Sūrah's Summary of the Middle**

The sūrah now steps out of the narratives to state, in its own voice, the *summary* of the *narrative middle*: *allādhī naḥī laka min akhbāri-l-amdāri* — "these are among the stories we have recounted unto thee of the towns." The *summary* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *seven stories* of the *middle* — *Noah, Hūd, Ṣāliḥ, Abraham, Lot, Shuʿayb, and Moses* — are *not a* *series of separate events*; they are *the stories of the towns* in the *sense of being* *the accounts that the sūrah has recounted to the Prophet*, in the *sense of being* *the unseen made speech in the form of the towns. The sūrah is stating, in the summary, that the *Makkans* are *hearing the summary of the stories* *in the Prophet's hearing*, and that the *hearing the summary* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *town* is *among the towns of the stories*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Prophet* is *not hearing the stories as a past event*; he is *hearing them as the unseen that God recounts to him*, in the way described in *verse 1's statement of the Book from the Presence of the Wise and Aware.

**"Among Them Are Some That Remain" — and the Word That the Sūrah Gives**

The *second* *clause* states the *first* *state* of the *towns*: *min-hā mā baqī wa-mā ḥaḍaḍ* — "among them are some that remain." The *bqā* — the *remaining* — is the *word* that names the *state of the towns that the sūrah does not destroy*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *towns* are *not all destroyed*, in the *sense of being* *the towns that the sūrah holds in the state of the remaining*, in the *sense of being* *the towns that the command does not reach. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *remaining* is *not a* *state that the towns hold by their own power*; it is *the state that the sūrah holds for them*, in the way described in *verse 48's statement of the communities for whom enjoyment is granted. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans'* *town* is *in the state of the remaining* at this *moment*, and that the *in the state of the remaining* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *not yet in the state of the mown down*, in the way described in *verse 3's statement of the appointed term.

**"And Some That Have Been Mown Down" — The Word That the Sūrah Adds**

The *final* *clause* states the *second* *state* of the *towns*: *wa-mā ḥaaḍ* — "and some that have been mown down." The *ḥaḍḍ* — the *mown down*, the *cut down* — is the *word* that names the *state of the towns that the sūrah destroys*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *towns* are *not all remaining*, in the *sense of being* *the towns that the sūrah holds in the state of the mown down*, in the *sense of being* *the towns that the command has reached and executed. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *mown down* is *not a* *state that the towns enter by their own choice*; it is *the state that the command holds for them*, in the way described in *verse 44's statement of the refrain struck over the wrongdoing people. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans'* *town* is *in the state of the remaining* but *within the range of the mown down*, and that the *within the range* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the same case* as the *towns of the stories*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — the *statement of the wronging* and the *seizing of the Lord* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *two states are not the end of the summary*, in the way described in *verses 101–102.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:101

> **We wronged them not, but they wronged themselves. And their gods whom they called upon apart from God did not avail them in the least when the Command of thy Lord came, and they increased them in naught but ruin.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"We Wronged Them Not" — The Sūrah's Statement of the Justice of the Account**

The sūrah now states the *principle* that *governs* the *stories of the towns*: *ma-ẓalamāhum* — "We wronged them not." The *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *destruction of the towns* is *not a* *destruction that the Lord performs in the state of the wronging*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the Lord holds in the state of the justice*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the sūrah states as the not wronging. The sūrah is stating, in the statement, that the *Makkans* are *hearing the statement of the not wronging* *applied to the towns of the stories*, and that the *applied to the towns* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *account* is *not a* *account that the Lord performs in the state of the wronging*, in the way described in *verse 4's statement of the One who is Powerful over all things. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* are *not* *faced with a Lord who wrongs*; they are *faced with the Lord who does not wrong*, in the way described in *verse 45's statement of the most just of judges.

**"But They Wronged Themselves" — The Word That the Sūrah Gives**

The *second* *clause* states the *word* that the *sūrah* gives with *particular* *weight*: *bal ẓalamū anfusahum* — "but they wronged themselves." The *word* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *destruction of the towns* is *not a* *destruction that the Lord inflicts upon them*; it is *the destruction that they bring upon themselves* in the *sense of being* *the act that the wronging of the towns holds upon the towns*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the refusal of the prophet produces upon the people. The sūrah is stating, in the word, that the *towns* are *not* *destroyed by the Lord without the wronging*; they are *destroyed by the wronging that they perform upon themselves*, in the way described in *verse 21's statement of the loss of the souls. The *commentators* *note* that the *towns* are *not* *destroyed by the Lord in the state of the injustice*; they are *destroyed by the failure to acknowledge the clear proofs and follow the teachings of the prophets*, and that the *failure* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *just*, in the way described in *verse 34's statement of the prophet's completion of his part. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* who *refuse the prophet* are *wronging themselves*, and that the *wronging themselves* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *account* is *not a* *account that the Lord performs in the state of the wronging upon them*, in the way described in *verse 109's statement of the share undiminished.

**"Their Gods Whom They Called Upon Apart from God Did Not Avail Them" — The Statement of the Idols' Impotence**

The *third* *clause* states the *statement of the idols' impotence*: *ma-dʿaw min dūni-llāhi lam yanfaʿū-hum* — "their gods whom they called upon apart from God did not avail them in the least." The *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *idols* are *not* *a protection for the towns*, in the *sense of being* *the objects that the towns called upon and did not avail*, in the *sense of being* *the objects that the command of the Lord reaches without the availing of the idols. The *commentators* *note* that the *reason* the *gods* *could not avail them* is that *in this world they could not bring about any benefit for them*, and that *this would be the case even more so in the Hereafter*, and that the *not bringing about any benefit* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *idols* are *not* *a protection in this world*; they are *not a protection in the Hereafter either*, in the way described in *16:20. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* who *call upon the idols* are *in the same statement*, and that the *in the same statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *idols* are *not a protection*, in the way described in *verse 21's statement of the forsaking of the fabricated.

**"And They Increased Them in Naught but Ruin" — The Closing That the Sūrah Gives**

The *verse* *closes* with the *word* that the *sūrah* gives with *particular* *weight*: *wa-zā-dū-hum illā taghāyub* — "and they increased them in naught but ruin." The *taghāyub* — the *ruin*, the *perdition* — is the *word* that names the *state* that the *idols* *produce* in the *towns*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *idols* are *not* *a protection that the towns hold without the ruin*, in the *sense of being* *the objects that the towns' calling increases in the ruin*, in the *sense of being* *the objects that the sūrah characterizes as the increase in the ruin. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Makkans'* *idols* are *not a protection*; they are *the increase in the ruin*, in the way described in *verse 16's statement of the deeds that come to naught. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verse* — the *seizing of the Lord* — is *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *increase in the ruin is not the end of the summary*, in the way described in *verse 102.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:102

> **Such is the seizing of thy Lord when He seizes the towns while they are doing wrong. Surely His seizing is painful, severe.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Such Is the Seizing of Thy Lord" — The Sūrah's Statement of the Seizing**

The sūrah now states the *form* of the *command* in the *summary* of the *stories of the towns*: *ka-dhālika khaḍhu rabbi idhā khaḍha-l-amdāra wa-hum ẓālimūn* — "such is the seizing of thy Lord when He seizes the towns while they are doing wrong." The *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *seizing* is *not a* *seizing that the Lord performs in a different form for different towns*; it is *the same seizing* in the *sense of being* *the act that the Lord holds in the state of the seizing*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the sūrah has stated in the five commands of the narrative middle. The sūrah is stating, in the statement, that the *Makkans* are *hearing the statement of the seizing* *applied to their own town*, and that the *applied to their own town* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *town* is *within the range of the seizing*, in the way described in *verse 49's statement of the accounts of the unseen. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Prophet* is *not hearing the seizing as a past event*; he is *hearing it as the form of the command that the sūrah states*, in the way described in *verse 1's statement of the determined and expounded Book.

**"When He Seizes the Towns While They Are Doing Wrong" — and the Word That the Sūrah Gives**

The *second* *clause* states the *condition* of the *seizing*: *idhā khaḍha-l-amdāra wa-hum ẓālimūn* — "when He seizes the towns while they are doing wrong." The *word* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *seizing* is *not a* *seizing that the Lord performs upon the towns in the state of the not wronging*; it is *the seizing that the Lord performs upon the towns while they are doing wrong*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the wronging of the towns holds in the seizing*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the sūrah characterizes as the seizing of the wronging. The sūrah is stating, in the word, that the *seizing* is *not a* *seizing that the towns can avoid by the not wronging*; it is *the seizing that the wronging holds*, in the way described in *verse 101's statement of the wronging of the towns upon themselves. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* who *do wrong* are *in the state of the seizing*, and that the *in the state of the seizing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *town* is *within the range of the seizing while they are doing wrong*, in the way described in *verse 100's statement of the towns that have been mown down. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *seizing* is *not a* *seizing that the Lord performs without the possibility of the not wronging*; it is *the seizing that the wronging holds*, and that the *wronging* is *the state that the towns can leave by the not wronging*, in the way described in *verse 117's statement of the Lord who would never destroy the towns unjustly while their people were reforming.

**"Surely His Seizing Is Painful, Severe" — The Closing That the Sūrah Gives**

The *verse* *closes* with the *statement* of the *quality* of the *seizing*: *inna khaḍhu-hu la-alīm shadīd* — "surely His seizing is painful, severe." The *alīm* — the *painful* — and the *shadīd* — the *severe* — are the *two qualities* that the *sūrah* *names* for the *seizing*, and the *naming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *seizing* is *not a* *seizing that the towns can experience without the pain and the severity*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the sūrah characterizes as the painful and the severe*, in the *sense of being* *the act that the five commands of the narrative middle hold in the quality of the painful and the severe. The sūrah is stating, in the naming, that the *Makkans* are *hearing the statement of the painful and the severe* *applied to their own town*, and that the *applied to their own town* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *seizing* — if the *Makkans* *continue in the wronging* — is *the painful and the severe*, in the way described in *verse 39's pairing of the punishment that disgraces and the punishment that endures. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing*, that the *next verses* — the *sign for those who fear the punishment of the Hereafter* — are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *seizing is not the end of the summary*, in the way described in *verses 103–104.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:103

> **Truly in that is a sign for those who fear the punishment of the Hereafter. That is a Day on which mankind shall be gathered together, and that is a Day that shall be witnessed.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Truly in That Is a Sign" — The Sūrah's Statement of the Stories as a Sign**

The sūrah now states the *purpose* of the *stories* for the *reader*: *inna fī dhālika la-āyah li-mann ikhāfa ʿadhāba-l-ākhirah* — "truly in that is a sign for those who fear the punishment of the Hereafter." The *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *stories of the towns* are *not a* *narrative for its own sake*; they are *the sign* in the *sense of being* *the evidence that the sūrah states for the one who fears the punishment of the Hereafter*, in the *sense of being* *the account that the fear holds in the sign. The sūrah is stating, in the statement, that the *sign* is *not a* *sign for all*; it is *the sign for those who fear the punishment of the Hereafter*, in the way described in *verse 23's statement of the humble before their Lord. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *Makkans* who *do not fear the punishment of the Hereafter* are *not* *in the state of the sign*, and that the *not in the state of the sign* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in the state of the not fearing*, in the way described in *verse 7's statement of the rejection of the resurrection.

**"That Is a Day on Which Mankind Shall Be Gathered Together" — The Word That the Sūrah Gives**

The *second* *clause* states the *Day* that the *sign* *holds*: *dhālika yawmu-yujmaʿu-l-nās* — "that is a Day on which mankind shall be gathered together." The *yawm* — the *Day* — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *has used* throughout for the *Day of Judgment*, and the *recurrence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Day* is *not a* *Day in the ordinary sense*; it is *the Day of the gathering* in the *sense of being* *the Day on which all mankind is gathered*, in the *sense of being* *the Day that the sūrah will state in 42:7 and 64:9 as the Day of Gathering. The sūrah is stating, in the recurrence, that the *Makkans* are *hearing the statement of the gathering* *applied to their own Day*, and that the *applied to their own Day* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *within the range of the gathering*, in the way described in *verse 4's statement of the return to God. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *gathering* is *not a* *gathering that the Makkans can avoid by their refusal*; it is *the gathering that the Day holds for all mankind*, in the way described in *verse 103's statement of the sign for those who fear.
